| **D199** | **A SETTLED RUN'S EVIDENCE RECEIPT BINDS TO THE SURFACES ITS DERIVATION CONSUMES, NOT TO THE DESCRIPTOR'S WHOLE SOURCE CLOSURE — AND FOR A RECEIPT THE CLOSURE HASH IS NEITHER NECESSARY NOR SUFFICIENT.** `buildRunEvidenceReceipt` STAMPED `descriptorFingerprint` FROM THE **CURRENT BUILD'S** `descriptor.contractFingerprint`, WHICH IS BOTH A FALSE STATEMENT (THE RECEIPT CLAIMED THE RUN EXECUTED UNDER WHATEVER TREE IS CHECKED OUT) AND THE REASON ANY EDIT ANYWHERE IN A ~31-FILE CLOSURE STRANDED EVERY ALREADY-SETTLED RECEIPT BEHIND `evidence receipt … already exists with different content`. CHUNK 2'S **FIVE** LIVE-PROOF `separations-kuali-read` RECEIPTS WERE STRANDED BY A RAW-CONTROL-BYTE FIX THAT CHANGED NO SEMANTICS, AND RE-RUNNING THE DOCUMENTS WAS NOT AVAILABLE — A READ WORKFLOW WHOSE IDENTITY IS THE THING IT READS RUNS ONCE PER SUBJECT. THE FIELD IS NOW THE RUN'S **OWN RETAINED** `envelope.contractFingerprint`, A FACT READ FROM AUTHORITY LIKE EVERYTHING ELSE IN A RECEIPT. D136 PROVED THE "NOT NECESSARY" HALF FOR PARKED WRITES; A RECEIPT ADDS THE OTHER DIRECTION — THE DECLARED REDACTION TABLE AND THE DECLARED CROSS-SOURCE OBLIGATIONS LIVE **OUTSIDE** EVERY DESCRIPTOR'S IMPORT CLOSURE, SO THEY MOVE WITH `contractFingerprint` BYTE-IDENTICAL (D197 MOVED ONE AND TWO RECEIPTS CHANGED CONTENT UNDER A FROZEN HASH). A HASH THAT CAN BE EQUAL WHEN THE ANSWER MOVED IS NOT A BINDING. RESOLVABILITY IS THEREFORE STATED DIRECTLY, ONE NAMED CHECK AT A TIME, AGAINST EXACTLY WHAT THE BUILDER READS: `workflow-identity`, `descriptor-version`, `canonical-input`, `evidence-graph`, `terminal-dependency-set`, `declared-evidence`. **`declared-evidence` IS THE ONE CHECK WHOSE BASELINE IS THE STORED RECEIPT ROW** — THE ONLY WAY TO SEE A DECLARATION THAT LIVES OUTSIDE THE CLOSURE — AND IT ATTRIBUTES EVERY DIVERGENT PATH TO THE ONE DECLARATION THAT OWNS IT (`declared-redaction`, `declared-details`, `declared-obligations`, `terminal-dependency-set`, `run-identity`, `receipt-composition`), SO A GENUINELY MOVED DECLARATION READS AS *WHAT MOVED* INSTEAD OF "THE BYTES DIFFER". `evidenceReceipts.insert`'s IMMUTABLE CHECK STAYS UNDERNEATH AS THE BACKSTOP AND IS NEVER SKIPPED. A REFUSAL PRINTS THE WHOLE TABLE WITH BOTH FINGERPRINTS, `[held]`/`[MOVED]`/`[skip]` DISTINCT, AN UNEVALUATED CHECK NEVER REPORTED AS HELD, AND **NO WAIVER, OVERRIDE, OR FORCE ARM**: A SETTLED RUN'S OPERATOR-FACING RECORD OF A REAL HR TRANSACTION IS NEVER OVERWRITTEN WITH A NEWER STORY ABOUT IT. CHUNK 2'S FIVE SPLIT EXACTLY ALONG THE FINDING — THE THREE WHOSE ONLY CHANGE WAS THE CLOSURE RE-DERIVE BYTE-IDENTICALLY AGAINST THE LIVE AUTHORITY DATABASE, AND THE TWO THAT ALSO CROSSED D197'S CLASSIFICATION FIX STAY FROZEN AND NOW SAY SO BY NAME. | ref operator-trust §2.3.3 (primary); ref operator-trust §2.3; ref write-safety §4.1 (D136, the same shape one layer over); `src/core/receipt-derivation-binding.ts`; `src/core/evidence-receipt.ts`; `tests/unit/receipt-derivation-binding.test.ts`; `tests/unit/architecture/evidence-receipt.test.ts` |
| **D200** | **A WORKER SWEEPS ITS OWN WORKER ID BEFORE ITS FIRST CLAIM, ON THE STRENGTH OF THE LEASE IT JUST WON — AND IT IS THE SAME SWEEPER, NOT A SECOND RECOVERY PATH.** `claimNext` **THROWS** WHILE A WORKER ID STILL OWNS A NON-TERMINAL RUN (DOC ref execution-and-sessions §0.3, ONE WORKER ONE ACTIVE ITEM), AND THE D118 SWEEP THAT WOULD RELEASE IT LIVES IN THE MAINTENANCE TICKER OF A **RUNNING** WORKER. THE TICKER'S BOOT BEAT ALREADY PRECEDES THE FIRST CLAIM, BUT IT IS **STALENESS-GATED**, AND THE GAP IS EXACTLY THERE: A WORKER WHOSE LOOP THROWS SETTLES ITS **LEASE** ON THE WAY OUT AND LEAVES ITS CLAIMED ROW WHERE IT WAS, SO THE NEXT PROCESS WINS THE LEASE SECONDS LATER AND INHERITS A ROW WHOSE HEARTBEAT IS FAR YOUNGER THAN THE 60s THRESHOLD — THE SWEEP SKIPS IT AND THE FIRST POLL THROWS `worker … already holds active run …`, CLEARABLE ONLY BY STARTING A **DIFFERENT** WORKFLOW'S WORKER OR CANCELLING THE RUN. NEITHER IS A RECOVERY PATH. **THE LEASE, NOT A TIMER, IS THE EVIDENCE:** `workerLeases.start` SUCCEEDED, SO NO OTHER PROCESS ANSWERS TO THIS WORKER ID, SO EVERY ROW STILL UNDER IT BELONGS TO A PROCESS THAT IS GONE WHATEVER ITS HEARTBEAT SAYS — A CLAIM A PROCESS MAY MAKE ABOUT **ITSELF AND NOBODY ELSE**, WHICH IS WHY THE REQUEST CARRIES A `workerId` SCOPE INSTEAD OF A WIDENED `staleBefore` (WIDENING WOULD RELEASE EVERY LIVE SIBLING WORKER'S FRESH CLAIM AT THE SAME INSTANT). `RunClaimSweepRequestSchema` NOW ADMITS **EXACTLY ONE** PROOF OF DEATH — `staleBefore` OR `workerId`, NEVER BOTH AND NEVER NEITHER — BECAUSE A `staleBefore` THE SCOPED SCAN QUIETLY IGNORED WOULD BE A LYING FIELD. **ONLY THE SCAN PREDICATE DIFFERS:** THE D118 WRITE-INTENT GATE, THE PARK/REQUEUE ROUTING AND THE TRANSITION LEGALITY CHECK ARE ONE CODE PATH, SO A RUN WHOSE ITEM HOLDS AN UNFINISHED `attempting` INTENT PARKS OR STAYS HELD AND **NEVER REQUEUES** — A STARTUP PATH THAT RE-IMPLEMENTED "RELEASE MY OWN ROWS" WOULD HAND A POSSIBLY-LANDED REAL HR WRITE BACK TO THE EXECUTOR. WHAT NO SWEEP MAY RELEASE IS **REPORTED, NOT ENDURED**: A `claimed`/`validating` ROW HOLDING AN ATTEMPTING INTENT HAS NO LEGAL PARKED-WRITE EDGE, SO STARTUP REFUSES NAMING THAT RUN AND DOC ref write-safety §4.1's TYPED OPERATOR RESOLUTIONS, RATHER THAN LETTING THE FIRST POLL THROW WITH NO REASON ATTACHED. THE PERIODIC SWEEP IS UNCHANGED — GLOBAL AND STALENESS-GATED, BECAUSE IT KNOWS NOTHING ABOUT WHO OWNS A ROW. | ref execution-and-sessions §3.0 (primary); ref execution-and-sessions §3 (D118); ref write-safety §4.1; `src/server/worker/compose.ts`; `src/infra/sqlite/run-store-contracts.ts`; `tests/unit/claim-sweep-recovery.test.ts`; `tests/unit/worker-composition.test.ts` |

IT CLASSIFIES NOTHING: "ALL TERMINATED" (S21), "ALREADY SEPARATED" (S22), "TWO CONCURRENT JOBS" (S23) ARE A PURE UNCONDITIONAL FOLD. LEGACY COMPUTES THE SAME FACTS AS **THROWS INSIDE ITS PAGE MODULE**, AND ONLY WHEN THE SEARCH HAPPENED TO RENDER A MULTI-ROW GRID — SO THEY LIVE ONLY IN A LOG AND A SINGLE-EMPLOYMENT PERSON WHO IS ALREADY TERMINATED IS **NEVER CHECKED AT ALL**, WHICH THE FIRST LIVE SUBJECT (EID `10001799`, ONE ROW, `Inactive`) TURNED OUT TO BE. A READ THAT REFUSES ON THE SHAPE OF ITS OWN ANSWER CANNOT SERVE A GATE WHOSE JOB IS TO SHOW THE OPERATOR THAT ANSWER. **THE PAGE IS PERSON ORG, NOT LEGACY'S `WF_JOB_SUMMARY.GBL`:** SAME FACTS, ONE GRID, ONE VISIT, ANCHORS ALREADY LIVE-VERIFIED — VERSUS TWO LAZILY RENDERED TABS WITH A FROZEN COLUMN ZIPPED BACK BY ROW COUNT. **ABSENT ARMS SPLIT BY MEANING:** EMPL RECORD / HR STATUS / BUSINESS UNIT ARE STRUCTURAL AND REFUSE A BLANK (A BLANK MEANS THE EXTRACTION READ A NON-EMPLOYMENT ROW); THE NINE DESCRIPTIVE CELLS CARRY `{state,reason}` (S24), SO AN UNREADABLE DEPARTMENT IS DATA AND THE CONSUMER THAT NEEDS ONE IS THE ONE THAT REFUSES. **ONE EXTRACTION, TWO PARSES:** `searchPersonOrg` KEEPS ITS EXACT PREFERRED-ASSIGNMENT DERIVATION (WHICH REFUSES A BLANK DEPARTMENT, BECAUSE A CANDIDATE WITHOUT ONE IS UNUSABLE) RATHER THAN BEING WIDENED, BECAUSE WIDENING IT WOULD MOVE A SCHEMA HASH SHIPPED RUN RECORDS BIND. | ref task-contract §2.11 (primary) · ref task-contract §2.8 (D194) · `docs/rebuild/migrations/separations.md` §9.5 · ref stores |
| **D202** | **THE ALWAYS-GATE IS BUILT, AND THREE THINGS CHANGED ON CONTACT — EACH STRONGER THAN THE DESIGN.** `separations-identity-check` IS DOC ref write-safety §14 AS A REGISTERED, LIVE-PROVED GRAPH: TWO UCPATH READS, A PURE COMPARISON PRODUCING THE DECISION PACKET + ITS DIGEST, THE OPERATOR GATE, A POST-GATE CHECK. **(1) THE PAYLOAD CARRIES NO ACTOR AND NO INSTANT.** §14.4 DREW `approvedBy`/`approvedAt` ON THE RESULT; THE KERNEL WRITES `resolvedBy = request.requestedBy` AND `resolvedAt = now` INTO `gate_results` IN THE SAME TRANSACTION THAT STORES THE RESULT AND RESUMES THE RUN, SO A CALLER-SUPPLIED ACTOR WOULD BE A **CLAIM BESIDE AUTHORITY** — D110/D114's SETTLED RULE. §14.6 RISK 6's HONEST STATUS: THE ROW ANSWERS IT, THE RECEIPT DOES NOT YET (A RECEIPT DERIVES FROM CHECKPOINTS AND A GATE HAS NONE), AND THAT IS OPEN FOR THE COMPOSITION. **(2) A TYPED EID THE PACKET NEVER SHOWED CANNOT BE APPROVED HERE** — §14.4 ADMITS ONE ONLY WHEN RE-VALIDATED AGAINST A LIVE READ, THIS CHUNK PERFORMS NO SECOND READ, SO IT REFUSES (`identity-approval-eid-not-shown`): NARROWER THAN §14.4 AND NARROW IN THE SAFE DIRECTION. **(3) `chosenFrom` IS CHECKED, NOT RECORDED** — A LABEL THE PACKET CONTRADICTS WOULD MAKE THE RECEIPT STATE SOMETHING FALSE ABOUT A HUMAN DECISION (D197 ONE LEVEL UP). **NO MODE DISABLES IT, STRUCTURALLY:** THE GRAPH HAS NO BRANCH AND NO `when`, `dryRun` REACHES ONLY THE WRITE SEQUENCER (EXECUTABLE ARM), A RESUME PAST AN UNRESOLVED GATE FAILS BY NAME, AND `identity-gate-before-commit` NOW AUDITS **BOTH SINKS** — EVERY TRANSACTION NODE *AND* THE TERMINAL RESULT — SINCE A READ-ONLY CHUNK'S PRODUCT IS THE `expected` SIDE OF A LATER WRITE'S BINDING. | **D203** | **A GATE'S DURABLE EVIDENCE IS A `gate_results` ROW, AND THE PROVENANCE COLLECTOR MOVES TO MEET IT — IT DOES NOT GET A FABRICATED CHECKPOINT.** READ-COMPLETION VALIDATION RESOLVES A TASK'S DECLARED `dependsOn` SET AGAINST WHOLE AUTHORITY CHECKPOINTS. A GATE HAS NONE AND NEVER WILL: ITS DURABLE RECORD IS AN **OPERATOR DECISION**, NOT AN OBSERVATION. SO A TASK DECLARING A GATE AS A DEPENDENCY WAS **STRUCTURALLY UNRUNNABLE**, AND EVERY FIXTURE MISSED IT BECAUSE NO FIXTURE HAD A TASK NODE DOWNSTREAM OF A GATE — THE FIRST LIVE GATED RUN FOUND IT, AFTER READING UCPATH, PARKING, AND TAKING A REAL OPERATOR APPROVAL THROUGH THE REAL COMMAND ENDPOINT. `upstreamEvidence` NOW CARRIES `gateResults` BESIDE `checkpoints`: A DECLARED DEPENDENCY IS SATISFIED BY EITHER, AN UNDECLARED ONE FROM EITHER SIDE IS REFUSED BY NAME, AND A GATE CONTRIBUTES **EXACTLY ONE** `operator`-SOURCED FACT DATED AT `resolvedAt` WITH **NO OUTPUT LEAVES** — WHAT A GATE DATES IS THE DECISION, NOT A FIELD. IT CANNOT MAKE A DERIVED OUTPUT LOOK FRESHER THAN ITS LIVE INPUTS, BECAUSE A DERIVED LEAF TAKES THE **OLDEST** ORIGINAL OBSERVATION AND AN APPROVAL IS ALWAYS AT LEAST AS NEW AS THE EVIDENCE IT WAS GRANTED ON. | **D206** | **ONE STORE, TWO SCREEN FAMILIES, TWO WRITE TRANSACTIONS — AND THE FIRST LIVE READ FOUND A CLOSED VOCABULARY THE CODE COULD NOT MATCH.** Chunk 4 adds a WRITE family to a store that already had a read family and composes **two transaction nodes in one graph**. (1) **THE STORE'S ONE DRIVER DECLARATION JOINS UNRELATED SCREEN FAMILIES** (D197 rule 1) — Person Org lives inside `#main_target_win0`, Smart HR renders **no iframe at all** — so the TYPE is joined while the schemas stay separate, and a fixture must satisfy both halves structurally while REFUSING the other's operations by name (a Person Org fixture that answered a termination scan would be inventing HR transactions). (2) **ONE RICH PAGE READ, TWO PROBE FOLDS**: ref write-safety §5's two probe kinds are two questions about the same rows, so `scanTerminationTransactions` classifies nothing and `termination-verdict.ts` folds it twice — D194/D201's shape applied to a WRITE's preconditions. (3) **A DELETE IS AN EXTERNAL WRITE AND GETS ITS OWN TRANSACTION**, composed BEFORE the filing with its own probe, verifier and key (the person alone): legacy's `deletePendingTransaction` is *the first mutation of the run* with no fence and no read-back (S30/S31), and hiding it inside a prepare would leave a crash window where the run had mutated UCPath while claiming to have staged nothing. (4) **THE SUBJECT BINDING IS THE NAME, AND IT MUST BE** — the Empl ID field holds the run's own input echoed back while `#PERSON_NAME_NAME_DISPLAY` is UCPath's own answer to it; legacy reads that name **nowhere** in the termination flow, which is the structural cause of the `T002173685` wrong-person termination. The live read made it stronger than the design: the anchor renders **zero nodes** until UCPath resolves a person, so the beat-③ observation and S48's `emplid-not-recognized` refusal are the SAME check. Legacy has no key for it, so it stays driver-internal rather than carrying a fabricated port row. (5) **A CLOSED VOCABULARY MUST BE READ OFF THE SURFACE THAT RENDERS IT, AND ONE SYSTEM MAY HAVE TWO**: `isUcpathTerminationAction` shipped testing legacy's `/Terminat/i` prefix, but the SS Smart HR search grid AND its transaction detail render the PeopleSoft code **`TER`** — only the *Transactions in Progress* grid renders `Terminatn`, and legacy uses both matchers in two modules without saying so. The first live read found a probe that would have answered `absent` about a person with a Pending termination (`T002209631`), which is the duplicate-filing failure the probe exists to prevent. `recoveryAbsence` is `operator-only` on BOTH commits because no propagation window has been measured — the controlled real commit stays open on §4f2's operator-named canary, and nothing was submitted. | ref task-contract §2.12 (primary) · ref write-safety §5 (both probe kinds) · `docs/rebuild/migrations/separations.md` §9.7 (the surface) and §9.8 (the live re-verification and the defect) · `src/stores/ucpath/CLAUDE.md` |

## Reconciliation round 57 (2026-08-14) — platform slice 2: the two blockers 6b's live pass surfaced

_phase: 3b_

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D214** | **A RUN *ATTEMPT* IS NOT ONE EXECUTION OF ITS GRAPH, SO THE CHECKPOINT IDENTITY CARRIES THE CLAIM GENERATION THAT EXECUTED THE NODE.** A gate park and its resume are two executions inside ONE attempt (§4 re-enqueues and the fresh claim keeps the attempt), and an `always-rerun` node re-executes across that boundary by design (§5.3) — so under `(workflow, item_id, node_id, attempt)` the second execution's genuinely different observation landed on the first execution's key and the immutable insert **refused it by name**, meaning a graph with an `always-rerun` node upstream of a gate **could not pass its own gate**. Chunk 6b found it live (`00419600` died one node past a real approval; `6a6c6f44` survived only because a killed worker bumped the attempt, and a crash is not a recovery path). `RunRecord.claimGeneration` ALREADY distinguishes exactly those executions — +1 per claim, never reset, at most one visit per node per claim — so nothing is invented. The three alternatives are refused on the docs' own grounds: writing NO row contradicts §5.3's "stored for audit" and strands D110/D114 evidence and D115 provenance; ADVANCING THE ATTEMPT rewrites an immutable envelope and re-labels a park as a retry; UPDATE or delete-and-reinsert is the overwrite D144/D199 exist to forbid. **Read forward (D133):** the field is optional, `checkpointLookupKey` appends nothing when absent, so every pre-D214 `record_key` stays byte-identical and resolvable — while a WRITER may not adopt that shape (`insert` refuses a record naming no generation, which is also what keeps SQLite's distinct-NULL UNIQUE honest). `latest` now means the latest EXECUTION; terminal evidence cites the execution the run consumed and authority re-reads that exact key, with the `not-run` contradiction check COUNTING every execution of the attempt instead of probing one; and the span path becomes `#<claimGeneration>.<taskAttempt>` so the projector's `(runId, attempt, spanPath)` fold and the timeline seed stop collapsing two executions into one (D123, one level in). Migration **v14** rebuilds `run_checkpoints` — a UNIQUE constraint cannot be widened in place — copying `record_key` verbatim and every historical generation as `NULL`. | ref workflow-model §5.7 (primary) · §5.3 · §6 · `src/domain/workflow/checkpoint.ts` · `src/infra/sqlite/migrations.ts` · `docs/rebuild/migrations/separations.md` §9.14 |
| **D215** | **A PREVIEW RUNS BEAT ① FIRST, BECAUSE THE REAL PATH DOES — THIS COMPLETES D210 RATHER THAN AMENDING IT.** Every sentence D210 ratified about how a previewed run ENDS still holds (outcome `done`, no `RunOutcome` arm, the terminal row bound to the preview schema, dependencies = the transaction node's own `dependsOn`, the receipt's `unknown` + load-bearing `DRY_RUN_PREVIEW`, `executeTransaction` still refusing a dry run by name). What was wrong is what the preview OBSERVED before it stopped: `executeDryRun` evaluated `prepare` with NO probe, and ref write-safety §5's probes are part of the transaction's safety story. Separations' `clear-pending` sweep made it consequential — for the ORDINARY subject, one with no pending transaction, the real run's probe settles `already-present` before a page is staged, while the preview staged a grid holding no row for the subject and REFUSED. **A rehearsal that fails where the performance succeeds is a false rehearsal**, and chunk 6b hit it on its first real document. The preview now runs the durable permanent-key lookup and then the policy-controlled live probe in the real path's order — both read-only by construction — **REPORTS** an `already-present` settlement instead of performing it (zero intent, zero checkpoint, zero run transition), and refuses on exactly the verdicts the real path refuses on (`attempting`, `parked`, ambiguous, unknown, foreign absent evidence, a present read over a settled-absent durable row); there is no arm that lets a preview answer where the real run stops. `TransactionDryRunPreview` gains a `probe` observation and an outcome union whose `would-prepare` arm OWNS `prepare` and `subject`, so a preview that staged nothing cannot spell an empty page; `transaction.previewed` gains the subject verdict `not-observed` for the same reason. The preview result's schema hash MOVES, which is a hash move and not a lie (D144) — the new hash describes the new bytes, and no preview had ever settled when this landed. No new table, no authority schema version. | ref workflow-model §2.1 (primary) · ref write-safety §5 · `src/domain/write-safety-primitives.ts` · `src/core/write-sequencer.ts` · `docs/rebuild/migrations/separations.md` §9.9/§9.14 |

|---|---|---|
| **D217** | **APPLYING A SETTLED MODEL TO TWO MORE FIELDS GROWS THE STAGED SET AND CHANGES NOTHING ELSE — AND WHAT A NEW FIELD COSTS IS THE EVIDENCE IT DOES NOT YET HAVE.** The operator answered the question chunk 5a queued and 5a's judgement 3 deliberately left unanswered — *"so what we are doing here is to collect all data first and then fill in the forms at the end"* — so legacy's **step-13** Kuali date write-backs (`Last Day Worked`, `Separation Date`) join §4e's ONE-pass staged set: **ten fields become twelve**, staged from the `dates` node's RECONCILED values, one save, one reload read-back over all twelve. Four things fall out. **(1) UNCONDITIONAL IS THE HONEST FORM UNDER ONE-PASS STAGING.** Legacy writes them only when reconciliation moved them, and that condition is a property of its INCREMENTAL drafts, not of this model: beat 4 compares every written field against the exact staged variable, so a conditionally staged field would need an expectation composed from *the page's own prior value* — the re-read beat 2 forbids — and the read-back would have to carry a second rule about which fields it may skip. When nothing moved the reconciled value EQUALS what the document carries, so the save writes the same string back and the read-back proves it either way. **(2) THE MOTIVATING CASE IS ON THE LIVE RECORD, NOT HYPOTHETICAL**: §9.14's doc 4524 moved `06/18/2026` → `07/03/2026` on a holiday, so the pre-amendment set would have filed a termination effective `07/04/2026` while the source document a human reads later still asserted `06/18/2026`. **(3) A NEW STAGED FIELD INHERITS NO EVIDENCE.** Both controls carry `exactly-one-visible` from the 2026-08-12 read-only pass and NOT D208's `exactly-one-editable`, neither `formKey` has ever been observed (so the table admits `null` and `kualiFinalizationFormKeysMissing()` names them — a fabricated key is a wire check that cannot fail), and **§4e's section caveat, DISCHARGED by 5c for the ten fields in three `edit` sections, RE-OPENS for exactly these two**, whose *Employee Information* section id nothing has read. All three are marked `// live-verify: next-live-pass` and listed in one place rather than waived — **and all three were DISCHARGED the same day by chunk 6b's read-only pass (§9.16): the section is `eTURtLdMM` and reports `edit` at both live steps, the keys are `ER1WhBzjcm` / `oEcp1xtQkj`, and both controls carry `exactly-one-editable` on two open documents. The two stay UNASSERTED on the wire, because a `Date` gadget's model value is epoch milliseconds and the staged value is `MM/DD/YYYY` — a captured key is not a captured shape**; the `stageText` read-back plus the twelve-field reload read-back are what make shipping them safe meanwhile, since a control that does not take the value refuses by name with zero fence and zero click. **(4) THE GATE NEEDED NOTHING.** D211 already carries `dates.lastDayWorked` / `dates.separationDate` INSIDE the approved packet, so the digest an approval binds to already covers the write-back; the two are NOT repeated into the packet's `document` block, because the packet is a presentation of decisions and one value rendered twice is a value that can disagree with itself. No node, edge, gate, `dependsOn` or permanent key moved. | `docs/rebuild/migrations/separations.md` §4e AMENDMENT (primary) · §9.15 (as-built) · §9.10/§9.12/§9.13 (addenda) · ref write-safety §14.2 (D211's packet) · `src/domain/ui/kuali-separation-finalization.ts` · `src/stores/kuali/CLAUDE.md` · ref stores · §9.16 (the discharge) |

Counts do NOT roll up (§10.1/D81: a badge counts exactly the rows its panel shows), harness runs keep their own labels (archived labels frozen, D179), and registration/startability/§3.2 obligations do not move — each harness still owes/holds its own controlled-commit proof, and no phase doc's exit criterion names rail presence. | ref workflow-model §1.2.1 (the module role) · ref dashboard-surfaces §9.9 (the presentation, primary) · `src/control/workflow-registry.ts` · `src/domain/dashboard/bootstrap.ts` · `src/dashboard/surfaces/workflow-category.ts` |

## Reconciliation round 62 (2026-08-14) — a retry is the item's next attempt

_phase: 3b_

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D221** | **THE CHECKPOINT KEY IS ITEM-SCOPED, SO THE ATTEMPT IN IT IS THE ITEM'S ATTEMPT — NOT THE RUN'S.** D214 closed the collision one ATTEMPT could hold; this closes the one one ITEM could. `run`/`retry` minted a new run for the same logical item born at attempt 1 / generation 1, so the retry's `always-rerun` nodes re-executed against the ORIGINAL run's attempt-1/generation-1 key and the immutable insert refused them by name — **a run that failed at or after its first `always-rerun` node could not be retried at all**, which for the composed `separations` graph is any failure at or after `timecard-search`. Chunk 6c found it live (`555ba4b4` retrying `12a5cd3e`, dead in 1.89 s; §9.17 item 8) and the only way forward was the re-read affordance, which mints a different logical item and is a different operator intent. **The counter is not invented**: `(workflow, item_id)` is the logical item this table is keyed by, §5.6 #1 has always said *"retry = new attempt"*, the DDL already annotates `run_id` as audit, and ref write-safety §5's write sequencer already reads `envelope.attempt > 1` as *"this item has been attempted before, so probe before you fence"* — a true reading only of an item-scoped counter. An enqueue therefore takes the item's next unspent attempt, read from authority (`runs.nextItemAttempt`) inside the transaction that inserts the run; a requeue advances the same counter rather than its own `+1`; and `runs.enqueue` REFUSES an attempt the item has already spent, so a stale read is loud at the insert instead of a collision three nodes later. D214's refusal of ADVANCING THE ATTEMPT still stands where it was made — a gate resume would rewrite an in-flight envelope and re-label a park as a retry — and neither reason reaches a freshly minted envelope for something that IS a retry; no-row and overwrite stay refused. **Nothing stored moves**: the key shape is unchanged, so every `record_key` stays byte-identical and there is no migration and no schema version. **Cross-run replay is preserved and is the point** — `latest`/`get` carry no run id, so a retry still replays the prior run's `replay:"checkpoint"` rows (pinned since Phase 1f by `run-executor.test.ts`'s cross-run retry case) — while `latest`'s `attempt DESC, claim_generation DESC` becomes a real order where two runs at attempt 1 were an arbitrary tie, and the receipt's `pickCheckpoint` and the D214 `not-run` contradiction count stop admitting a sibling run's rows. | ref workflow-model §5.7 (primary) · §5.6 #1 · `src/domain/run/envelope.ts` · `src/infra/sqlite/authority-database.ts` · `src/core/command-kernel.ts` · `src/core/run-fan-out.ts` · `docs/rebuild/migrations/separations.md` §9.17 |

## Reconciliation round 63 (2026-08-18) — the backup hazard belongs to the inbox

_phase: 3a_

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D222** | **A STALE OR MISSING BACKUP IS A `storage` NOTIFICATION, NOT AN EVERY-VIEW BANNER — AND THE FIRST WIRED PRODUCER OF §2.6'S TRIGGER IS THE BACKUP-HEALTH WATCH.** Operator decision on the live surface: *"things like these should be under notifications, not in the dashboard page."* The plan already agreed — ref dashboard-surfaces §2.6 has routed "storage degradation … failed backup" onto the `storage` trigger since D79b, and D137/D138 built the durable actor-keyed inbox and 2i-B its bell — but **no production caller ever published a trigger**, so the every-view `StorageBanner` was carrying the backup hazard the inbox was built for. The dashboard composition (the backup cadence's owner, 2i-D) now observes the SAME cheap backup summary the bootstrap serves — one extracted `readBackupSummary`, so the bell can never announce a state Settings would not show — and publishes `storage-degraded`/`backup` on the 15-minute tick. Three bounds: a **transition** into or between `stale`/`missing` publishes and a standing state does not, because the D134 fold marks a row unread again and a per-tick publish would make the row unreadable-forever (the spam §2.6 forbids); a **failed publish forgets nothing** — the notifier advances its memory only after the durable row commits, so the next tick retries, and the failure is retained on the doctor wire's `service` arm rather than swallowed; **recovery publishes nothing**, because the trigger union has no "recovered" arm and inventing one is a new decision. The banner narrows to the unreachable-authority state for a STRUCTURAL reason: the inbox is an authority table, so an authority that cannot be written cannot record a notification about its own death — the banner is the one surface that can still say it (it still appends the backup sentence when both hazards are true). The gear's attention dot and Settings are unchanged. | ref dashboard-surfaces §2.6 (primary, as-built block) · ref dashboard-surfaces §2.5 (the cadence owner) · `src/services/notifications/storage-health.ts` · `src/server/dashboard/compose.ts` · `src/dashboard/surfaces/settings.tsx` |

_phase: 3a_

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D224** | **THE QUEUE ACTION BAR IS AN ISLAND, NOT A FULL-BLEED BAND — THE SHELL IS MADE OF CARDS AND THE BAR WAS THE ONE ELEMENT THAT WAS NOT.** Operator decision on the live surface ("you should also have this bar as like an island like the other parts"). Two consequences ride along and both are improvements rather than separate decisions: **the inner padding becomes `--ds-space-cozy`**, which is the same 12px `--ds-shell-inset` resolves to, so the leading control aligns with the queue panel's own header text instead of with the window edge; and **the bottom hairline goes**, because a bordered island already states where it ends. D18's three groups, their order, the no-wrap rule and the middle group's sideways scroll are all unmoved. **The reference corpus is NOT re-frozen in this commit** — the working tree was concurrently carrying another slice's descriptor edits, so every `queue-day` reference was already drifting on `descriptorHash` before this change touched a pixel, and folding somebody else's in-flight work into a reviewed corpus is exactly what D132's "one deliberate switch" exists to prevent. ## Reconciliation round 65 (2026-08-18) — the timeline is categorical

_phase: 3a_

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D225** | **THE RUN-DETAIL TIMELINE IS CATEGORICAL: A SEGMENT IS A DECLARED CATEGORY, NOT A NODE.** Operator ruling — the composed `separations` run drew eighteen truncated segments plus a gate and duplicate resume pairs, so the surface that exists to say what a run is doing said nothing. A descriptor carries an optional `timeline: [{ label, nodes }]`; the factory derives the frozen projection (member labels resolved through the same `labelOf` the step projection uses) and REFUSES a declaration that is not exhaustive — a node left out, named twice, undeclared, or a duplicate category label each throws at `build()`, because a node missing from the declaration would vanish from the timeline silently. A descriptor declaring nothing gets one category per node, unchanged for `person-lookup` and every chunk harness. The fold is SERVED (`TimelineWireSchema.steps`): state folds loudest-first, duration sums measured executions, a re-execution across a gate resume becomes `×N` rather than a second segment (D214's two-executions-in-one-attempt, one level up), and a gate takes its state from the run's gate records with no duration — the wait belongs to the gate and the gate belongs to its category. `steps` is optional for D133 read-forward (a pre-D225 archived record, a replayed workflow with no registered descriptor) and `events` is still served in full: the strip is a presentation of that history, never a replacement. Separations' six approved categories: Kuali extraction · Identity check · Job summary · Kronos timecard · UCPath transaction · Kuali finalization, with legacy's `transaction-check` folded into the third-to-last because here the pending check IS the sweep transaction plus the sequencer's probes. Known gap with an owner: the parity fixture composes its wire directly and serves no `steps`, so the corpus still photographs the per-event fallback. | ref dashboard-surfaces §9.3 (D225, primary) · §1.1 · `docs/rebuild/migrations/separations.md` §9.18 |

_phase: 3a_

_D227–D229 are reserved by this round; each lands with its normative text in its owning doc._

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D229** | **THE EXPLORER'S ⓘ OPENS WITH THE DESCRIPTOR'S OWN WORDS.** A descriptor carries `summary` (one sentence about the workflow) and `nodePurposes` (one per node), plain data on the broad view beside `subjectProjection`/`timeline`; a declared node-purpose list is EXHAUSTIVE, refused by the factory otherwise, for D225's reason. The wire carries an `absent` arm naming the file that owes a sentence rather than throwing — the explorer serves every workflow in ONE document, so refusing the surface over missing prose would take the operator's index of the system down — and the new `explorer-prose-declared` guard makes that arm unreachable for a REGISTERED workflow by failing the build. Nothing composes prose from a label. | ref operator-trust §5.9 (primary) · `tests/unit/architecture/explorer-prose-declared.test.ts` |
| **D228** | **A TASK NODE'S `when` IS A DECLARED CONDITION, NOT AN OPAQUE CLOSURE.** A closed flat vocabulary (`is-true`/`is-false`/`equals`/`not-equals`/`is-present`/`is-absent`) over an operand that is the run's canonical input or a declared upstream node's output. `evaluateNodeCondition` is the ONE evaluator and `describeNodeCondition` is a total projection of the same value into words, so the explorer's sentence cannot drift from the rule the executor applies — the authored `whenLabel` ref operator-trust §5.7 recorded is refused precisely because it would be a second description. Four refusals replace silent wrong answers: only the presence arms tolerate a missing value, a path through a non-object throws, a non-boolean under `is-true` throws, and `defineWorkflowDescriptor` refuses a path no schema owns or an upstream node the condition does not depend on — at module load rather than on the one run that finally reaches the node. No `all-of`/`any-of`: a condition the union cannot express is a compile error, and widening it is a reviewed code change. | ref workflow-model §3.3 (primary) · ref operator-trust §5.9 · `src/domain/workflow/node-condition.ts` |
| **D227** | **THE EXPLORER'S NODE PANEL IS A FIELD TABLE, AND ITS CENSUS IS DERIVED FROM THE CONTRACT'S OWN ZOD SHAPE.** `Field · System · Recorded`, with the census walked off the same schema object the arm's `inputSchemaHash`/`outputSchemaHash` fingerprints, an array counted as one leaf, a union contributing the union of its arms, an unrecognised zod tag refused by name, and `Recorded` carrying the field's declared PROVENANCE (never a run's value) with a reachable `unknown` arm. | ref operator-trust §5.9 (primary) · `src/validation/contract-boundary.ts` · `src/domain/dashboard/explorer.ts` |

## Reconciliation round 68 (2026-08-19) — the queue row says what it is and what it made

_phase: 3a_

_D230–D231 are minted by this round; each lands with its normative text in its owning doc._

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D230** | **A RUN'S ORDINAL IS DURABLE, PER-WORKFLOW, AND ASSIGNED AT ENQUEUE.** The queue footer's `#N` cannot be derived from a row's position in a DAY's page — that gives the same run a different number on a different day, and a number an operator quotes in a support conversation that means something else tomorrow is worse than none. `RunRecord.runNumber` is therefore durable, taken from `nextWorkflowRunNumber` inside the transaction that inserts the run (D221's `nextItemAttempt` one scope up), so a fan-out of forty takes forty consecutive numbers and a retry takes the next rather than inheriting the retried run's. Schema **v15** promotes it to a mirrored `tasks.run_number` column with `UNIQUE (workflow, run_number)`, because both properties the ordinal must have are SQL properties; `enqueue` refuses a spent number by NAME above the index, since a constraint violation names an index and not the mistake. Read forward, never back-filled (D133): every pre-v15 row keeps NULL, NULLs are distinct under SQLite's UNIQUE, and the integrity audit's comparison is null-safe. A GAP is legal (a refused fan-out entry leaves nothing behind) and a REPEAT is not. It is not identity — nothing joins on it. | ref dashboard-surfaces §9.2 (primary) · ref frontend-backend-integration §3.2 · `src/domain/run/record.ts` · `src/infra/sqlite/migrations.ts` |
| **D231** | **A QUEUE ROW SAYS HOW IT IS CONTAINED, AND CHIPS ONLY THE CHILDREN IT DOES NOT COUNT.** `containment` is `root | member | linked`, PROJECTED from the run's own shape and lineage (ref frontend-backend-integration §1.1's *never inferred from a workflow id*), and a schema refinement refuses every way it, `lineage` and `surfaceKind` can disagree — the load-bearing arm being `member`, because `surfaceKind` is what the count filter reads and a row claiming membership while presenting as a single run would be counted in its parent's rollup AND in the day's totals. There is deliberately **no `rejected` arm** though the §2 vocabulary names one: a submission the parent could not turn into work never becomes a run (`fanOutQueuedRuns` refuses the whole fan-out inside the caller's transaction; a start-plan refusal rides the plan as data), so no queue row exists for it to describe, and it lands with the first producer that writes such a row. `linkedRuns[]` carries each delegated child's id, workflow, label, panel and LIVE STATUS resolved through the same status function every row's own status comes from — bare ids would make the client re-derive a status from the day payload, which is D81's second path and goes blank the moment the target sits on another day. The member and linked partitions are disjoint BY CONSTRUCTION: the rollup counts only `operation-member` children, so no child is both counted and chipped. `memberPreview` is composed from the member rows' own titles so a group can never name somebody it does not contain. | ref dashboard-surfaces §9.2 (primary) · ref frontend-backend-integration §3.2 · `src/domain/dashboard/projection.ts` |

## Reconciliation round 69 (2026-08-19) — the row draws what it says, and a name is a value

_phase: 3a_

_D232–D233 are minted by this round; each lands with its normative text in its owning doc._

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D232** | **THE PALETTE BUDGET BUYS A FIFTH SEMANTIC HUE, AND SPENDS IT ON `dry run`.** Four hues (info / success / warning / danger) had no honest home for the rehearsal chip: `info` is what the `test` chip beside it is drawn in — the one pair on a queue row a glance has to separate — and every amber token in this product means *a human is involved*, which a rehearsal is not. `--ds-rehearsal-*` is therefore a REAL light/dark pair, not an alias of the parked amber the demo's own `violet` tone resolves to; that collision is the one deliberate colour deviation from the demo on this row. It is named for what it MEANS rather than for its colour, because `--log-violet` was a hue name on a semantic token and was later re-derived to amber for a different meaning — the name outlived the colour and now lies about it. No `-solid-*` step: this hue is only ever a chip, and a solid fill would be a fifth voice in the loudness ladder. The same hue draws the same fact on the Log Panel's provenance bar — one meaning, one colour. | ref dashboard-surfaces §9.2 (primary) · `src/dashboard/ds/tokens.css` |
| **D233** | **A RENAME IS A DESCRIPTOR-DRIVEN CONTROL, AND EVERY RUN VERB CASES THE CALLER'S VERSION.** `rename` had been a kernel command since Phase 1c with no way to send it: its absence from the served vocabulary was recorded as *"an inline field on the row"* and no build ever grew that field, so §10.3's display name was writable only from a CLI this runtime does not have. It joins `ActionCommandKeySchema` and is served at `menu` on every status — `executePresentation` has no state precondition, so a run that has already written to UCPath can still be named, which is exactly what makes a rename safe: it touches the subject, the trace and the ordinal not at all. The one narrowing is row SHAPE, not a kernel fact: a member row loses it, because `displayName` replaces the title on the row it rides and naming a member would put free text where the group's list prints the person that item is about. The dialog is intercepted from the descriptor as the identity ceremony is, and the composer REFUSES a rename with no name rather than posting a plausible one. The second half is the CAS: `executePresentation` compared against its OWN re-read, which cannot fail, so a stale rename or hide applied silently and reported success. The premise it was built on is about STATE, not version — **a name is a value the operator authored**, so applying yesterday's over today's discards an edit while answering "Recorded." All three presentation verbs now CAS `request.target.expectedVersion` like every other run verb; hide keeps the discipline because a CAS rule with one exception is a rule nobody can check. The guard covering that arm had been asserting the hole under the heading *"scopes CAS to the real race families"*, and its registered invariant moved with it. | ref dashboard-surfaces §9.2 (primary) · ref dashboard-surfaces §10.3 · `src/core/command-kernel.ts` · `src/domain/dashboard/actions.ts` |

## Reconciliation round 70 (2026-08-19) — there are runs and archived runs

_phase: 3a_

_D234 is minted by this round; it lands with its normative text in its owning doc._

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D234** | **THE QUEUE'S MODEL IS RUNS AND ARCHIVED RUNS; THE HIDE/UNHIDE PAIR IS RETIRED.** Operator ruling, dated: *"there are runs and archived runs; there should be no hidden runs."* `unhide` could never have been SERVED — a hidden run was filtered out of the queue projection, so no row existed to hang it off, and no build ever grew a hidden-runs surface — so the reversible half of a *"reversible presentation tombstone"* was unreachable from the product, and the row's own confirm copy admitted it (*"the row cannot be brought back from here"*). A one-way door dressed as a tombstone is worse than no door. Both kernel arms, both schema keys, the served vocabulary, the descriptor policy, the client composer's fall-through and the row descriptor all go; so does the hidden-run exclusion, which the projection had been applying THREE times (patch notifier, day wire, Activity report). Archiving (D179/D80) is now the ONE whole-run exclusion, applied once and inherited by every surface, which is what makes D81's count story checkable. The durable `RunRecord.hidden` is a PARSE-TOLERATED VESTIGE, not a deletion: the schema is strict and every stored row carries `"hidden": false` inside its `record_json`, so dropping the key would make the authority database refuse to open — D133's read-forward rule pointing the other way — and there is no migration because the field was never a column. It becomes `optional()`, written by nothing, read by nothing, and an absent value means nothing. Production held zero flagged rows when this landed. `wire-connectivity` was structurally blind to this defect (it audited kernel↔schema symmetry, not whether a served key is ever PROJECTED) and gains a third direction that CALLS the row producer and scans the section producer's comment-stripped literals — comments stripped because `unhide` appeared in the projector only inside the prose explaining why it is never served. `executeBump`'s CAS hole is closed in the same pass on D233's rule, with no new number. | ref dashboard-surfaces §"D234 — the queue's model is runs and archived runs" (primary) · ref dashboard-surfaces §2.4 · `src/core/command-kernel.ts` · `src/domain/run/record.ts` · `src/server/dashboard/projectors/run-queue-projection.ts` |

## Reconciliation round 71 (2026-08-19) — a batch answers run by run

_phase: 3a_

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D235** | **A BULK COMMAND IS N CAS-BOUND CHILDREN AND ONE PER-RUN VECTOR — NEVER ONE AGGREGATE "DONE".** The batch verbs are `cancel` and `retry` (operator ruling 2026-08-18; `bump` is schema-admitted and deliberately not offered). The server door is `POST /api/v1/commands/bulk`, a second route because N commands answer N results; each child runs the SAME command-service path a single press reaches, with its id DERIVED from `bulkCommandId` + the run id so a re-delivered envelope replays the same N children (`replayed: true`) instead of filing N more. Vector arms: `applied` / `conflict` (server's `currentVersion`) / `refused` (the single-press `CommandRejectionCode`) / `errored` (verbatim, and the pass STOPS — `requestedCount` makes the untried remainder legible). Whole-request refusals reach no target: empty set, a run named twice, and the unexecuted `all-or-none` mode, refused by name. Client half: OFFERED (≥1 selected row serves the verb) vs ELIGIBLE (the intersection, versions frozen at composition) are stated separately; skipped rows are counted and named; a plan with nothing eligible refuses to become an envelope; the result band renders the vector run by run with subjects frozen from the sent plan. | ref dashboard-surfaces §"D235 — a bulk command is N children and one vector" (primary) · ref dashboard-surfaces §2.4 · `src/server/dashboard/commands/command-service.ts` · `src/dashboard/app/bulk-dispatch.ts` |

## Reconciliation round 72 (2026-08-19) — the report's second authority, its dropped tile, and its export

_phase: 3a_

_D236, D239 and D240 are minted by this round; each lands with its normative text in its owning doc. (D235 and D237–D238 are held by concurrent branches and are not this round's.)_

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D236** | **THE ACTIVITY REPORT'S WRITE-LEDGER BLOCK IS SERVED, AS AN EXPLICIT D81 CARVE-OUT.** Operator ruling: the demo's last Activity block — *"Write ledger — what was actually filed"* (System / Entries / Production / Test instance, the test count tinted amber, and the Well empty state) — joins the served report. Every other Activity total is pinned to the ONE run/queue projection, and this one cannot be: the ledger is a different authority — append-only, never pruned, never archived (§10.2 rule 1's *"archiving is a display lifecycle; the ledger is an audit one"*) — so **its entries outlive the runs that filed them**, and a window whose runs have all been archived can still hold lines that folding from the run corpus would report as *"nothing was filed"* about real HR transactions. D81 forbids two answers to ONE question, not a second question. The exemption is narrow and bounded rather than a widening: the block is reported BESIDE the totals and the wire's refinements assert NO relationship to `totalRuns` or any status bucket (a cross-check would claim the two corpora cover the same rows, which is the claim that is false) — the one refinement is per row, `entries === prod + test`, where both numbers come from the same authority. The seam is stated ON the surface, in the block's own subtitle and in `activityReportCaveats`, because a second authority the reader cannot see is one the reader assumes is the first. The fold REUSES the archive's `LedgerReadThrough` rather than adding a reader (two parse disciplines over an audit file is one wrong answer about what was filed), closes its window on each entry's own `confirmedAt` through the projection's own local-day rule while treating the UTC partition file name as nothing but which files to open, and THROWS on a line it cannot read — the pointer path can answer one citation with a reason, a fold has no such column. The empty state is served now that the axis is, having been an assertion before it was. `one-projection-counts` grows the bound from both sides: a block may be BUILT only in the domain contract + the read-through and ASKED FOR only in the read-through + the one projection, by exact set equality, and the behavioural arm files real ledger lines into the seeded world and requires every count to stay byte-identical. | ref dashboard-surfaces §10.1 "D236 — the write-ledger carve-out" (primary) · ref frontend-backend-integration §6.7 · `src/domain/dashboard/activity.ts` · `src/infra/ledger/ledger-read-through.ts` · `tests/unit/architecture/one-projection-counts.test.ts` |
| **D239** | **THE HOURS-SAVED TILE IS DROPPED; NO OPERATOR-ENTERED MINUTES STORE IS BUILT.** Operator ruling, and a DECISION rather than a deferral — there is no code to point at, because the tile was never rendered. The demo's fifth headline tile multiplies a hand-kept `MANUAL_MINUTES` constant by the runs that finished; `docs/rebuild/findings/activity.md` recorded it decide-first with three questions attached (where the minutes live, who may change them and whether changes are versioned, the wire shape), and the answer is that none of them get answered. Every other number on this report is MEASURED. A minutes-per-run figure would be the only one that is not, and it would sit on **the one artifact in the product that leaves the operator's screen** — now literally, since D240 exports it — where a plausible total gets quoted in a meeting and never traced back to the constant it came from. So `ActivityWorkflowTotal` carries no `minutesPerRun`, `ActivityReportWire` no `hoursSaved`, and `Tile` keeps no `estimate` tone or badge, because nothing else wears them. The trigger that re-opens it is NAMED: a real minutes source — a measured baseline of the manual process, or a durable operator-owned store with an owner, an edit path and versioning so a re-run of an old range cannot silently restate history. The finding CLOSES with the decision rather than being deleted, so the argument survives the next person who notices the demo has five tiles and the product has four. | ref frontend-backend-integration §6.7 "D239" (primary) · `docs/rebuild/findings/activity.md` |
| **D240** | **THE ACTIVITY REPORT IS EXPORTABLE, AND THE RULES TRAVEL WITH THE NUMBERS.** `GET /api/v1/reports/activity.csv?from=&to=` serves the same `ActivityReportWire` the panel renders, as a file, and the header gains the demo's Export button beside the ⓘ. It refuses a half-named window in the SAME words as the JSON route — D180's argument gets stronger for an export, not weaker, because this copy is the one that gets mailed on. The condition ref frontend-backend-integration §5's `export if later served` was waiting on is the whole shape of the build: **an exported report with the numbers and without the rules is the lying artifact this surface's design prevents**, so the caveats are a BLOCK of the artifact ahead of every total they qualify, not a footnote — the panel can put them one press away because the ⓘ is on the same screen, and a CSV has no ⓘ. They are the SAME sentences, and the error rate the SAME rounding: `activityReportCaveats` and `activityErrorRatePercent` moved into the browser-safe contract and both consumers read them, because two copies of a rule is the same lie with a delay on it. Absences export their REASONS (a spreadsheet will average a zero and cannot average a sentence), an unregistered workflow exports its reason rather than an id dressed as a label, the ledger block restates its own D236 seam because the exported copy is read without the panel's subtitle beside it, and a cell is quoted when it must be and never REWRITTEN — prefixing a leading `=` to defuse a formula parser would put a different string in the file than the one on screen, and the artifact's entire value is that the two agree. The operator id and app build are deliberately absent: they are client props that never crossed this wire, and a server composing them would assert provenance about a reader it cannot see. No success toast either — the download settles after the handler returns and nothing in the surface can observe whether it arrived. | ref frontend-backend-integration §6.7 "D240" (primary) · ref frontend-backend-integration §5 · `src/server/dashboard/queries/activity-export.ts` · `src/domain/dashboard/activity.ts` |

## Reconciliation round 73 (2026-08-19) — every workflow has a short name, and the crash-test dummy leaves the rail

_phase: 3a_

_D241 and D242 are minted by this round; each lands with its normative text in its owning doc. (D235 and D237–D238 are held by concurrent branches and are not this round's.)_

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D242** | **THE SHORT CODE IS ONE DECLARATION WITH TWO CONSUMERS — UNIQUE AT THE COMPOSITION ROOT, AND FROZEN INTO AN ARCHIVED RECORD BESIDE THE LABEL.** Operator ruling: an archive row must read `sep v1`, not `Separation Documents v1` — at 800 rows the long label costs the name column real width, and the Activity per-workflow table and the version registry want the same token. `docs/rebuild/findings/workflow-taxonomy.md` recorded the need as *"no short code exists anywhere in the rebuild — not on the descriptor, not on the wire"*, and that premise was **wrong in both halves**: `WorkflowDescriptor.code` has existed since Phase 1 and the bootstrap wire has served it since 1g-spine — it was simply invisible, because its ONLY consumer was `deriveTraceId`, which composes `<code>-<HHMMSS>-<runId4>`. So the honest build is a unification, not a field: minting a display code beside the trace code would have given one workflow **two short names**, with nothing telling an operator that `sd-141203-9a1c` in a log and `sep v1` in the archive name the same thing. `WorkflowCodeSchema` becomes the ONE definition (`src/domain/workflow/presentation.ts`), bound by the descriptor factory, the bootstrap wire and the explorer wire so the shapes cannot fork the way separations 6c's parity twins did; it widens from exactly-two alphanumerics to **two-to-four lowercase LETTERS** — letters because a code sits immediately in front of a digit run in every trace id and `s1-000619-4ebf` reads as one opaque blob, and the bound widens NOW because widening it after a stored trace id exists means every reader accepts both shapes forever. **Every existing code is KEPT** (`pl`, `sp`, `sd`, `si`, `st`, `sf`, `sk`, `kt`): each is already the leading segment of real stored run rows, spans, notes and ledger lines, and ref operator-trust §2.5's `explain run <traceId>` is a real lookup path — renaming would spell one workflow two ways across the operator's own evidence trail, buying legibility the labels already provide. UNIQUENESS is enforced where the whole registry is in scope (`auditWorkflowCodes` / `workflowCodeRegistry`, fail-loud at LOAD, the same audit §1.4 item 1 runs), because two workflows sharing `sd` would make a stored identifier name two things and an archive row lie about whose work it is — neither of which throws anything. `TraceIdSchema` stays deliberately WIDER than the writer's rule (`[a-z0-9]{2,4}`): it parses durable stored ids, so it is a reader, and a reader narrowed to today's writer rule refuses history the moment that rule moves (D133). The archive FREEZES the code beside the label at generation 3 — resolving it later would be a descriptor read on the archived path, which is what charter §25 forbids — with the D179 absence union for a workflow already unregistered at sweep time, and a record written before generation 3 reports its absence NAMING its generation rather than guessing. | ref workflow-model §1.1.1 (the code, primary) · ref dashboard-surfaces §10.2 (the frozen archive field + the surfaces) · `src/domain/workflow/presentation.ts` · `src/control/workflow-registry.ts` · `src/domain/archive/model.ts` |
| **D241** | **A PROVING WORKFLOW GROUPS BY ITS ROLE, UNDER `TEST EQUIPMENT` — NEVER UNDER AN HR CATEGORY.** Operator ruling: *"a crash test dummy is not a workflow … only the main workflows show up in the workflow rail … maybe it belongs in a different category, perhaps in test."* D220 answered the rail half and stopped there, so `kuali-timekeeper-annotation` still headed the Run Modal's picker and the Explorer under **Operations**, beside daily HR work — the half the operator was actually looking at. The defect is a category answering the wrong question with a plausible answer: `category` says *which HR area does this touch*, and for a workflow whose whole purpose is carrying D135's controlled-transaction proof that is not false, it just does not say the thing you need before starting a run — and a plausible wrong answer is worse than none, because nothing prompts anybody to check it. So the ONE grouping rule (`groupWorkflowsByCategory`) reads the MODULE's role for these entries and not their category: `proving` groups under **`Test equipment`**, appended after every listed and unlisted category, on every surface that lists one. It is lifted OUT of the category pass rather than added beside it, so one workflow can never appear twice under two meanings; the group DROPS when nothing proving is served, exactly as an empty category does; and the rail is behaviourally unchanged because it already filters to `standalone` + `composition`, so the new group arrives empty and drops. The picker's AUTO-PICK set narrows to that same pair on D219's own argument one level out — a dialog that must not open on a step the operator did not ask for must not open on test equipment either — while the workflow stays REGISTERED and startable from its own panel and from the `Test equipment` group, because a standing safe re-proof of the write machinery that nobody can run is not a standing proof. The heading lives beside the category order and the nesting rule, never as a rail-side id list: a hardcoded id is precisely the workflow-specific customization the role model was built to avoid. | ref dashboard-surfaces §9.9 (D241, primary) · ref workflow-model §1.2.1 · `src/dashboard/surfaces/workflow-category.ts` · `src/dashboard/surfaces/run-start.tsx` |

## Reconciliation round 74 (2026-08-19) — the browser-profile wedge was never about the profile

_phase: platform_

_D243 and D244 are minted by this round; each lands with its normative text in its owning doc._

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D243** | **THE WEBAUTHN COUNTER IS A SHARED RESOURCE WITH UNSYNCHRONISED WRITERS, AND A REFUSED DUO CEREMONY IS AN OBSERVATION.** The "browser-profile wedge" (separations §9.17 item 5, §9.20 item 6) is a signCount regression, live-bisected in §9.21: the same profile logs in at 19.14 s with its stored counter ahead of Duo's view and burns the full 60.01 s bound with the counter forced 1 063 behind it, nothing else changed, with Duo's own page reading *"Your login request using your security key was canceled or timed out."* Duo's counter is per CREDENTIAL and one credential covers every UCSD system; Duo Autopilot's copy is per BROWSER PROFILE and ref execution-and-sessions §1 partitions profiles per `(worker, system, instance, configFingerprint)` — so N writers share one counter and none can see another's copy. `autoImportPackagedCredential` already knew this and stamped a cold profile's counter to unix `now` *"monotonic across fresh browser profiles"*; the SIGN path advanced by `+ 1`, as if the counter were private. So a cold profile JUMPS the shared counter to now while a warm one creeps by one, and a warm profile cold-stamped earlier can never catch up: every subsequent login on it is refused, permanently, with profile deletion the only exit — which is exactly the remediation both prior records arrived at without knowing why it worked. `duo-autopilot/src/sign-count.js` now floors every ceremony at wall-clock seconds (`max(stored + 1, nowSec)`), the one source every profile shares. The second half is the runtime's own: a login must REFUSE on Duo's rendered refusal rather than wait its landing bound out, because the landing bound (`duoApprovalSeconds`, 180 s) is larger than the acquisition envelope (`sessionAcquireMs`, 60 s), so the envelope always fires first and `describeStalledLanding` — the function whose whole job is explaining a stalled Duo login — is **unreachable at the shipped defaults**, handing the operator D125's message naming Duo hand-approval, a real cause and never this one. §4.1's rule applies to the refusal as it does to the proof: Duo says so in words about a second after the assertion, so the recipe observes it. A negative control keeps a merely-slow prompt reporting the old message, because a fix that collapses two Duo failures into one is the same mis-attribution pointed the other way. | ref execution-and-sessions §4.2 (primary) · `duo-autopilot/src/sign-count.js` · `src/infra/browser/ucsd-sso.ts` · `tests/unit/duo-sign-count.test.ts` |
| **D244** | **A VENDORED RUNTIME DEPENDENCY'S LOAD-BEARING RULE IS PINNED BY THE REPOSITORY'S OWN GATE.** `duo-autopilot/` is not a sample: `assertDuoAutopilotReady` refuses to launch a browser without it, so every live run of every workflow depends on its arithmetic. Its own offline lane (`duo-autopilot/npm test`) is not part of `npm run test:coverage`, so a rule pinned only there is a rule no per-commit gate checks — the "a dead arm cannot stand in for enforcement" argument, one repository boundary out. D243's counter rule therefore lives in ONE tiny chrome-free ESM module with a sibling `.d.ts`, so `tests/unit/duo-sign-count.test.ts` imports and pins the real production function rather than a restatement of it. This is the narrow shape and not a licence to reach into vendored code generally: it applies to a rule the runtime's correctness depends on, extracted so it can be imported, never to a text audit of a file the program cannot type. The pin's load-bearing case is the two-profile simulation — the defect is invisible to any single-writer example, which is why `+ 1` survived from the extension's first day. | ref execution-and-sessions §4.2 (primary) · `duo-autopilot/src/sign-count.d.ts` · `tests/unit/duo-sign-count.test.ts` |

## Reconciliation round 75 (2026-08-20) — the runtime's own name

_phase: platform_

_D247 is minted by this round; its normative text lands in ref guards-and-tests §3.13 and the root `CLAUDE.md`
(Commands, Operational isolation)._

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D247** | **THE LAUNCH COMMANDS ARE PLAIN, AND A COMMAND'S NAME IS NO LONGER AN ISOLATION RESOURCE.** `rebuild:cli` / `rebuild:dashboard` / `rebuild:dashboard:prod` / `rebuild:dashboard:web[:build|:preview]` become `cli` / `dashboard` / `dashboard:prod` / `web` / `web:build` / `web:preview`. Operator ruling: the `rebuild:` prefix dated from the plan to grow this runtime INSIDE the legacy checkout; since D93 this is its own repository, and a prefix naming the other repo was the last thing in the tree still defining this one relative to it. What STAYS hard: every other row of `config/runtime-isolation.json` — state/artifact roots, ports, process-lock root, browser session namespace — and the exact-script DRIFT check on both sides (`dashboard` in this checkout must be byte-exactly the declared command, and the legacy checkout's declared commands are audited the same way). What is RETIRED: the distinct-command-NAME arm of `operational-isolation`, whose rationale was a muscle-memory `npm run dashboard` in the wrong checkout. That slip now starts the other repo's dashboard on its own port under its own roots and locks, which the surviving rows make a harmless, legible mistake rather than a collision — the arm was guarding a name, and names are not what the two runtimes share. | ref guards-and-tests §3.13; `CLAUDE.md` |

## Reconciliation round 76 (2026-08-19) — the two honesty gaps on the run panel

_phase: 3a_

_(Minted as round 71 on its branch; renumbered at merge — rounds 71–75 were taken by concurrently landed work.)_

_D237–D238 are minted by this round; each lands with its normative text in its owning doc. (D235–D236
are minted concurrently by sibling slices of the same wave.)_

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D237** | **A SETTLED GATE'S PANEL NAMES THE VERDICT, THE APPROVER AND THE INSTANT — OFF THE DURABLE ROW, NOT OFF THE SPAN KEY.** `run-detail`'s `resolved` gate arm was keyed on the span stream's `resolutionKey`, and the command service emits the literal `resolved` for EVERY gate (deliberately, with a comment naming the display-key deferral) — so the approve/reject arm was **structurally unreachable** and every settled gated run's panel reported the gate ABSENT with a reason that reads like a data problem, while `resolvedBy` had sat in authority since D202 and the receipt had read it since D204 (separations §9.17 item 7a). The verdict now comes from `authority.gateResults.listByRun` through D204's own declared arm table, with `resolvedBy`/`resolvedAt` off the row the kernel wrote inside the resolving transaction — the SAME derivation the receipt uses, so the two surfaces an operator reads side by side cannot name two different humans. `resolvedBy` is REQUIRED on the arm, which is safe exactly because the arm was unreachable: no served wire and no archived record can hold a `resolved` gate that predates it. Panel and receipt diverge on ONE thing, deliberately: the receipt REFUSES to derive over an undeclared gate or an unlisted arm value (it is the durable artifact), while the panel — which must still render the run it explains — turns each such gap into a NAMED absence, including the one the span stream can create alone (a folded `gate.resolved` with no durable row, stated as that divergence so a lost decision never reads as a workflow that asked for none). The panel draws the settled decision as a record in the run's own stream at `resolvedAt`; the archived Decision card names the approver too. | ref operator-trust §2.3.5 (primary) · ref dashboard-surfaces §9.3 (the presentation) · `src/server/dashboard/queries/run-detail.ts` · `src/domain/dashboard/run-detail.ts` · `docs/rebuild/migrations/separations.md` §9.17 item 7 |
| **D238** | **A REHEARSAL'S TERMINAL OUTCOME IS NEVER `verified-done`.** `done → verified-done` was mechanical, so the composed separations DRY RUN that reached its preview terminal wore the label this product reserves for an independently verified REAL completion — while the receipt for the same run said `partial`/`unknown` with a load-bearing `DRY_RUN_PREVIEW` warning, and the authority schema itself carries `CHECK (result <> 'verified-done' OR confidence = 'verified')` (D210's named carry-forward; observed on a settled preview at separations §9.17 item 7b and again at §9.20). A terminal `done` run whose own envelope asked for a dry run now takes **`partial`** — exactly what `buildRunEvidenceReceipt` derives for it — with `rehearsal: true` beside it. **No new confidence tier and no sixth result**: the enum and the confidence vocabulary are unchanged, and `rehearsal` only restates the envelope fact D6 already puts on the queue row. A wire refinement makes `verified-done` beside `rehearsal` UNREPRESENTABLE. The field is `z.literal(true).optional()` — absence is the only other spelling, so `false` would be a second one, and optional is D133 read-forward for an archived detail frozen before it existed. Only the `done` arm carries it: a dry run that failed or was cancelled reached no preview terminal and `failed`/`cancelled` already say what happened. The panel's outcome bar leads with the word (`dry run · partial · 09:20`), because `partial` alone reads as a run that half-worked; the sentence is an exported pure function so it is pinned by a test rather than only by the parity corpus. The REAL `verified-done` path is untouched. | ref dashboard-surfaces §9.3 (D238, primary) · ref operator-trust §2.3.5 · `src/domain/dashboard/run-detail.ts` · `src/dashboard/surfaces/log-panel.tsx` · `docs/rebuild/migrations/separations.md` §9.17 item 7 |

## Reconciliation round 77 (2026-08-20) — the parked write gets a way in and keeps its way out

_phase: 3b_

_(Minted as round 75 on its branch; renumbered at merge — 75 was taken by concurrently landed work.)_

_D245–D246 are minted by this round; each lands with its normative text in its owning doc, ref write-safety
§4.1. (D230–D244 are minted by concurrent slices of the same wave and land in their own rounds.)_

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D245** | **THE CONFIRMED-PRESENT ARM SERVES ITS PROOF CONTRACT, SO AN OPERATOR CAN COMPOSE ONE.** `resolvePresent` refuses any proof whose `schemaHash` is not the commit contract's own and then parses the value through that exact schema — and the parked-write section served **neither the hash nor the shape**, so no client could compose either half. The arm was drawn DISABLED on every parked run in the build (`proof-contract-unserved`), leaving a write that DID land exactly two exits: record it **absent**, which is a durable lie that authorizes a second real submit against a live HR system, or leave it parked forever. The copy also told the operator to *"resolve this write from the CLI"*, and no run-mutation CLI has ever existed — `POST /api/v1/commands` is the only mutation door. The section now carries `proofContract`: the commit task, the completion kind, the exact `schemaHash` a resolution must echo, and the proof schema DESCRIBED as an ordered list of typed fields (`src/validation/proof-shape.ts` — literals the schema pins, constrained strings with their own pattern, closed option lists, bounded integers, booleans, addressed by dot path through one nested level). Both halves are derived by the composition root beside `gateNodes`, on that view's own stated rule: the BFF queries never reach the descriptor builders, and a query that recomputed a schema hash would be a second copy of the contract's identity. **Nothing is guessed and nothing is pasted**: a shape this build cannot describe answers with a NAMED `unavailable` reason and leaves the confirmed-ABSENT arm reachable, because a free-text JSON box is a client authoring a durable record about a real HR transaction with only its own opinion of the shape. The form PRE-FILLS NOTHING — the run holds what it TRIED to write, and offering that back as the proof is §14.6's rubber-stamping shape one control over — and the operator does not assert the schema's literal `source`, which names which page shape the fields come from; who vouched is recorded where it already was, as `proofSource: "operator-attestation"` on the outcome and the ledger entry, with the evidence note retained in the command journal. Client-side refusal is per field and EARLY over the constraints a wire can carry; a leaf carrying a `.refine()` is served `serverOnlyRules: true` so the surface says the server checks more than the box does rather than implying its own tick is the verdict. And a proof that does not fit is now a typed `rejected`/`schema-mismatch` rather than a throw the dialog could only report as a transport failure — `executeResolveGate` has answered exactly that for the identical situation one command over since the gate landed. | ref write-safety §4.1 (primary) · `src/validation/proof-shape.ts` · `src/domain/dashboard/run-detail.ts` · `src/server/dashboard/compose.ts` · `src/dashboard/app/proof-form.ts` · `src/dashboard/surfaces/park-resolve.tsx` |
| **D246** | **CANCEL IS REFUSED WHILE A FENCED WRITE IS UNRESOLVED — "CANNOT ALTER THE INTENT" WAS TRUE AND WAS NOT THE WHOLE RULE.** §4.1 has said since Phase 1c that cancel may move the run but not the intent, and `executeCancel` honoured it exactly. What nothing said is what happens NEXT: a cancelled run is terminal, `executeWriteResolution` refuses every lifecycle that is not `parked-write`, and `buildParkedWritePayload` throws for any other state — so the cancel took **both** typed resolutions off the surface with it and left a durable record saying "this run is over" about a real HR transaction nobody had settled. Cancel was not leaving the intent alone; it was orphaning it, and the operator was WARNED about the wrong half (the confirm copy admitted the outcome stayed unknown, and never said it would stay unanswerable). The kernel now rejects `cancel` (`invalid-state`) when any run in the cascade holds an intent that is `attempting` or `parked`, naming the run, system, intent, generation, status and §4.1, and cancelling NOTHING — the same shape D118's sweeper, D200's boot refusal and D179's archive `apply` already take over an unresolved possible-submit. `attempting` is refused too and is the worse case: that run is not `parked-write`, so no section describes the intent at all. The rejected alternative — keep the resolutions reachable for a cancelled-but-parked run — is a contradiction rather than a wire change: `resolvePresent` settles through beat ⑦ with a `resume-queued` transition and `resolveAbsent` calls `runs.resume`, both illegal on a terminal run, so it would mean either resuming a cancelled run (against ref dashboard-surfaces §2.4 and against the copy the operator agreed to) or letting a COMMITTED real write land on a run recorded as cancelled, which is a worse record than the defect. Ordering the two acts costs one step and keeps every durable statement true. | ref write-safety §4.1 (primary) · `src/core/command-kernel.ts` · `src/server/dashboard/projectors/queue-row-actions.ts` |

## Reconciliation round 78 (2026-08-20) — the Session Panel stops guessing what a worker is

_phase: 3a_

_D248–D249 are minted by this round; each lands with its normative text in its owning doc. (D250 and
later are minted by concurrent slices of the same wave.)_

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D248** | **A WORKER CARD IS THE AUTHORITY LEASE, NOT AN INFERENCE OVER CLAIMED ROWS.** The projection derived a worker's phase from two observations — a worker span that ended meant `stopped`, a claimed row meant `working`, anything else meant `idle` — which is precisely the inference ref execution-and-sessions §3 says the durable lease exists to make unnecessary (*"the Session Panel needs worker phase without inferring it from whatever rows happen to be claimed"*). It cost three of the six state arms the panel drew: a daemon that had taken its lease and not yet reported read as `idle` (the demo's own "calling it idle misreads capacity"), a paused worker looked untouched, and a worker whose PROCESS DIED was drawn identically to one the operator had asked to stop — so the bar's "N workers need attention" chip could never fire. `WorkerCardWireSchema` now carries `workflowId`, `state`, `directive`, `startedAt`, `endedAt` + `stopReason`, `browsers[]`, `leaseVersion` and `revision`, composed in `server/dashboard/projectors/worker-cards.ts` from the lease joined with the worker's own span fold. The card SET is authority's: every live lease is a card, a settled lease is a card while the retained span stream still names its worker, and a worker the spans name but authority does not is not a card. `state` and `directive` stay SEPARATE fields for the reason `src/domain/run/worker.ts` states in its own header — a command records what was asked, the worker records what is true — and every arm of both has a producer, which is why the lifecycle's unwritten `paused`/`stopping` states are not mirrored and the demo's `waiting` arm is GONE (§1 gives every worker its own browser session per system, so there is no cross-worker lease to wait on and nothing could name a holder). `browsers[]` is the sessions a worker HOLDS, closed on the span that closes them and empty on a settled card; it carries no `health` and no `url`, because nothing probes a live session and nothing records a page address, and a six-arm health enum with one reachable arm is the orphan §8.8 exists to catch. `leaseVersion` is the lease's own row version and never `revision`, which counts folded spans. R7's remaining port lands on these fields in the same change: the card names its WORKFLOW (joined from the bootstrap list the Workflow Panel already draws from, never a second served label) with the served `queued` count beside it, the trace id or the server's own absence reason on the subline, the browser tile grid, the elapsed clock — dated by the SERVED instant every other elapsed on screen uses, because a browser `Date.now()` would be a second uncalibrated clock and the demo ticks its own only because it has no server — and the demo's crashed-card arm for a `failed` lease with its recorded reason underneath. | ref frontend-backend-integration §3.2 (primary) · ref execution-and-sessions §4 · `src/domain/dashboard/projection.ts` · `src/server/dashboard/projectors/worker-cards.ts` · `docs/plans/2026-08-13-detail-fidelity-audit.md` R7 |
| **D249** | **`STOP` REACHES THE LEASE, AND THE THREE CONTROLS BESIDE IT NAME WHY THEY DO NOT.** `Stop` posts `stop-worker` through the ONE command route with the served `leaseVersion` as its `expectedVersion` — the field the audit refused to bind while `revision` was the only version on the wire, because binding a span counter would have fabricated the fact a version check checks. It is composed by `composeStopWorkerCommand`, which stands BESIDE `composeCommandRequest` rather than inside it: that composer is exhaustive over the served row-action vocabulary and every arm of it targets a `runId`, so widening the shared target to hold a worker lease would make every run arm accept a target it can never use. Three demo controls beside it stay absent with named causes rather than being drawn from nothing: the lease-wait block (no producer — see D248), the browser context menu (`refresh-browser`/`reopen-browser` are real kernel verbs whose process effects the dashboard supervisor refuses by name), and the `+` / AddWorkersDialog (`spawn-worker` refuses the same way, and no pool version is served). | ref frontend-backend-integration §3.4 (primary) · `src/dashboard/surfaces/shell.tsx` · `src/dashboard/app/command-dispatch.ts` · `docs/plans/2026-08-13-detail-fidelity-audit.md` R7 |

The active-run policy's **CONTROL is refused and its FACT is drawn**: `planStart` reads `descriptor.enqueue` and `StartPlanIngress` carries no per-start override, so the demo's select would offer an answer the server would ignore — group 01 draws the declared value read-only in the demo's slot, over a table exhaustive across `EnqueuePolicy` that states what the PLANNER does rather than what an arm's name suggests, including that **`supersede-active` supersedes nothing** (no planner path implements it, no descriptor declares it — both pinned).
