The method TABS are **not built and not deferred**: the demo renders them only above one method, `uploadRun` is declared by no registered workflow, and a strip over a set of one is D186's sample-of-one. | ref frontend-backend-integration §"D251" (primary) · `src/dashboard/surfaces/run-start-model.ts` · `src/dashboard/surfaces/run-start.tsx` · `src/dashboard/app/dashboard-app.tsx` · `docs/plans/2026-08-13-detail-fidelity-audit.md` ledger |

## Reconciliation round 80 (2026-08-20) — the Data ledger names its step, and the ledger's last rows get their dispositions

_phase: 3a_

_D252–D253 are minted by this round; each lands with its normative text in its owning doc, ref frontend-backend-integration.
(D248–D251 are minted concurrently by sibling slices of the same wave.)_

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D252** | **A LEDGER VALUE NAMES ITS STEP, ITS DIRECTION AND THE BOUND IT IS HELD UNDER — ALL THREE DECLARED, NONE INFERRED.** The Context rail's header recorded two axes as unservable: *"nothing durable says whether a recorded field was read or written"* and *"the originating system is still absent"*. Both sentences are true of the `run_checkpoints` ROW and false of the descriptor that produced it — the same false-deferral shape D251 found on the picker — because the producing node declares a task contract whose `effect` is ref task-contract's own word for what it does to the world, and the store that implements that task declares the system. `ContextDataSectionSchema.entries[]` therefore grows two OPTIONAL, read-forward blocks: `origin` (`{nodeId, step, dir, system?}`) and `freshness` (`{maxAge, override, feeds[]}`). Nothing is guessed — an entry with no field identity draws neutral ink and NO arrow, because defaulting to `read` would claim the run changed nothing; a headless store contributes no system rather than a label. The bound is resolved through **`resolveFreshnessBound`**, the function `auditCheckpointFreshness` itself calls, and `feeds` through `reachableCommits`, so the sentence an operator reads is the rule the executor applies; an unbounded policy travels as the WORD, because `JSON.stringify(Infinity)` is `null` and a deliberate bound would arrive as a missing one. The schema REFUSES a bound with no origin. The view is composed at the root beside `gateNodes`/`writeContracts` and reads the MODULE, not the descriptor, because a task's system belongs to its store and a descriptor may not reach one (D106). The rail groups by `origin.nodeId` and never by label (two steps may record a field of the same name), and prints the bound only where it can BITE — an empty `feeds` set refuses nothing, so it says nothing. `staged`/`unconfirmed` stay undrawn for a STRUCTURAL reason rather than a wire gap: a `prepare` contract is `checkpoint: "in-memory-only"` so a staged value is never the checkpoint a row reads from, and an unconfirmed write is a `parked-write` run whose transaction node has no checkpoint at all — ref write-safety §4.1's park section is its honest surface. | ref frontend-backend-integration §"D252" (primary) · `src/domain/dashboard/run-detail.ts` · `src/server/dashboard/compose.ts` · `src/dashboard/surfaces/context-rail.tsx` · `docs/plans/2026-08-13-detail-fidelity-audit.md` ledger |
| **D253** | **A REFUSED COMMAND THAT LOOKS LIKE A SAVE IS THE WORST ARM TO DROP, AND TWO SURFACES DROPPED IT.** `CommandResultSchema` has carried a `conflict` arm since the kernel's first command — a CAS refusal with the server's `currentVersion` — and the Data rail's save and the notification bell's mark-read both branched on `rejected` alone, so a conflict fell through to the SUCCESS path: the rail closed its editor, discarded the operator's drafts and reported saved, for a correction the kernel never applied. That is the silent-wrong-"submitted" this program exists to prevent, reached without a single fallback being written — an unhandled union arm is a fallback nobody typed. Both surfaces name it now, with the server's own `currentVersion` in the sentence and the drafts kept, and `settleCommandResult` is the shared fold, held by the `command-result-exhaustive` guard, so a third consumer cannot repeat the omission. The demo's per-field CAS re-offer stays UNBUILT and is recorded rather than deferred: the kernel refuses at the run's version and re-reads nothing per field, so a per-field re-offer would need `executeEditCheckpoint` to project each patched leaf's server-current value out of its refusing transaction — a change to what the kernel returns, named as the trigger. | ref frontend-backend-integration §"D253" (primary) · `src/dashboard/surfaces/context-rail.tsx` · `src/dashboard/surfaces/top-bar.tsx` · `src/dashboard/app/command-result.ts` · `docs/plans/2026-08-13-detail-fidelity-audit.md` ledger |


## Reconciliation round 81 (2026-08-20) — the dead channel goes, and the findings ledger closes

_phase: 3a_

_D254–D255 are minted by this round; each lands with its normative text in its owning doc, ref dashboard-surfaces._

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D254** | **A VOCABULARY ARM NOTHING CAN EMIT IS UNREACHABLE, NOT UNUSED — SO `span.patched` IS RETIRED (operator ruling).** The detail channel carried a declared per-workflow key set (`descriptor.details`), an emit-time key validator, a projector fold into `row.details`, and the Context rail's read of it — and it had zero production emitters in the strongest available sense: the kernel's `SpanEmitter` never carried the arm, so no path in `src/` could produce one; only fixtures ever did; and the real state root holds no such line. Every surface it fed rendered its empty state permanently, through every green gate, since 1g-spine. Removing it is reversible and honest; building the emitter was work nobody asked for. `descriptor.details` STAYS with its meaning restated — no longer a patch-key vocabulary but a DECLARATION with three live readers (the receipt's mandatory-leaf set, the run-detail data-origin bound, the Explorer's field census) — so the removal is scoped to what the dead channel alone fed. The Context rail's membership is now one rule, D252's made structural: a row exists iff a `capabilities.editData` field has a stored checkpoint, and `editableEntryOf` takes that checkpoint as a PARAMETER, which deletes a checkpoint-less arm the fold alone could reach. Serving declared details from checkpoints is the named alternative, rejected as a BUILD whose membership question D252 answered two days earlier; the trigger that re-opens it is an operator asking for a declared non-correctable value on the rail, and the answer then is a checkpoint read, never a re-added event arm. | ref dashboard-surfaces §1.1 "D254" (primary) · `src/events/types.ts` · `src/infra/events/span-jsonl-writer.ts` · `src/server/dashboard/projectors/run-queue-projection.ts` · `src/server/dashboard/queries/run-detail.ts` · `docs/rebuild/findings/dashboard-projection.md` |
| **D255** | **A CLOSE BINDS TO AN OPEN SPAN, AND THE RECORD NAMED THE WRONG PRODUCER.** The task-span fold bound a `span.ended` to the FIRST entry at `(attempt, spanPath)`, so a repeated identity did not undercount — it fabricated: entry one kept its own start and took execution two's end, reporting a duration that spans both executions and the idle gap between them (a gate park is hours), while execution two stayed open and was dropped as unmeasured. The close now binds to the last STILL-OPEN entry and appends rather than overwriting when every entry at that identity is closed, so each execution stays a fact about itself (D123/D214 at the fold). The record's stated cause — a requeue re-executing a node at one span path — was FALSIFIED: `taskSpanPath` has carried the claim generation since D214. The reachable producer is the write sequencer's beat spans (`<trace>/<node>/<beat>`), which D214's identity fix never reached; that gap is DEFERRED with its trigger and its shape (the repair is `<nodeSpanPath>/<beat>`, not a `#<gen>` suffix, which would attribute the span to a node named `prepare`), because a transaction node currently cannot execute twice inside one attempt — and where it could, the failure lands first as the writer's in-process "opens exactly once" throw, not as a chip. | ref dashboard-surfaces §2.3 "D255" (primary) · `src/server/dashboard/projectors/run-queue-projection.ts` · `src/core/write-sequencer.ts` · `docs/rebuild/findings/dashboard-projection.md` |

## Reconciliation round 82 (2026-08-20) — the dashboard's clock ticks, and the open panel follows its run

_phase: 3a_

_D256–D257 are minted by this round; each lands with its normative text in its owning doc, ref dashboard-surfaces
§"Wave-2 connection-proof presentation deltas". They close two of the three defects Track C's live
connection proof recorded against this doc (`docs/rebuild/migrations/separations.md` §9.22 items 2 and 4);
the third — the structurally always-on shape hazard — is a defect against a contract this doc
already stated, so it is ratified as an as-built correction in the same section and mints no
decision. (D258–D260 are minted concurrently by sibling slices of the same wave.)_

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D256** | **A LIVE ELAPSED TICKS, FROM ONE CALIBRATION AGAINST THE SERVER'S INSTANT.** `useBootstrap` fetches once, and `boot.serverClock.now` was the ONLY instant the queue row's timer, the gate-age pill, the session footer and the worker cards computed against — so every live elapsed was frozen at page load: a parked run read **`0s` for over two minutes** (the tab loaded before the run started, so the difference was negative and `elapsedSecondsBetween`'s clamp made it a plausible zero) and a reload made the same row jump to `2m 54s` and freeze again. The server's instant stays the authority (a browser wall clock is what D124 forbids substituting); the browser contributes only the MONOTONIC time since that instant arrived, which no NTP step can move. One module holds the one calibration `(servedInstant, monotonicMs at receipt)`, derives `now` on a one-second tick and on `visibilitychange`, and `dashboard-app.tsx` calls it ONCE and passes the value down every path that carried the frozen one — including the keyboard order's sort key, and including the notification bell, whose own `systemClock.now()` was the last display-time wall-clock read in the bundle. A backwards elapsed is now DRAWN with its sign rather than clamped, because "the clock is behind the fact" must not wear "this just happened"'s shape; a sub-second skew still renders `0s` on its own. Re-calibration from a served heartbeat is NOT built and names its trigger: the BFF is a loopback singleton, so both clocks are one machine's, and what buys the wire field is ref frontend-backend-integration §6.9's deferred exposed path (D189). Parity is held by pinning the other half — the lane freezes `performance.now` before navigation and VERIFIES the freeze (D187's rule on a clock), which makes the derived instant exactly the fixture's served one and every existing reference byte-stable. | ref dashboard-surfaces §9.9 (D256, primary) · `src/dashboard/state/served-clock.ts` · `src/dashboard/surfaces/format.ts` · `src/dashboard/app/dashboard-app.tsx` · `tests/parity/helpers/app-driver.ts` |
| **D257** | **THE OPEN RUN-DETAIL PANEL FOLLOWS ITS RUN, OFF THE DAY STREAM'S OWN REVISION.** `useRunDetail` refetched only on `runId` or `detailNonce`, and the nonce was bumped ONLY by a dispatched command or an applied Data edit — never by SSE. So a panel left open through a run's progress froze on the wire as of the operator's last click: after the gate approval its header read **`Queued`** beside a queue row reading **`Done · took 8m 20s`**, and since `receipt` enters `availableSections` only at terminal, **the evidence receipt was unreachable from a panel you had open**. The follow key is the run's OWN served `rowRevision` — already on the day wire, already the CAS target of every served action, and it moves exactly when the run moves — so nothing polls and a run this day does not hold (archived, another day) is not followed because it cannot move. It tracks CHANGES rather than a difference against the loaded detail: the two legitimately disagree (authority vs projection, one routinely a beat ahead) and a comparison would refetch in a loop. The decision is a pure fold — `adopt` on a run switch, `settle` after a short window on a move, `hold` otherwise — so a burst of heartbeat revisions coalesces into one read while a switch does not wait. The sections follow for free through `usePanelSections`'s existing `rowRevision` key, and neither half blanks on a re-read of the SAME run: a panel that flashed a spinner every time its run advanced a node would be unreadable for exactly the runs worth watching. | ref dashboard-surfaces §9.3 (D257, primary) · `src/dashboard/state/use-run-detail.ts` · `src/dashboard/app/use-panel-sections.ts` · `src/dashboard/app/dashboard-app.tsx` |

## Reconciliation round 83 (2026-08-20) — the projection stops claiming a run's history it cannot see

_phase: 3a_

_D258–D259 are minted by this round; each lands with its normative text in its owning doc, ref dashboard-surfaces.
(D256–D257 and D260 are minted concurrently by sibling slices of the same wave.)_

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D258** | **A TIMELINE EVENT ID CARRIES THE OCCURRENCE ORDINAL AT ITS SEED, READ FORWARD — AND ONE POISONED RUN WAS BLOCKING EVERY ARCHIVE.** Run `00419600…` / `sp-080639-0041` (2026-08-14) holds two `span.started` and two `span.ended` at the identical `(attempt 1, sp-080639-0041/timecard-search#1)`: a **pre-D214** gate resume re-executing an `always-rerun` node, both executions emitting the same span path because the path did not yet carry the claim generation. D255 taught the FOLD to keep them apart; the id mint still derived one `noteId` for both, so `RunDetailWireSchema`'s `timeline note ids must be unique` refused the whole wire and that run's detail answered HTTP 400 — and because a version bump is ONE atomic act over every prior-version run and the archive freezes the record through this same projection, that single unreadable run refused `apply-version-bump` with `SCHEMA_INVALID` for the whole workflow, with no per-run skip available and none wanted (skipping contradicts D179's "the archive holds the bytes the operator last saw"). The seed now appends `~<n>` for the *n*-th occurrence at that seed, first occurrence appending nothing — D214's own read-forward shape, so every id already derived and every id an archived record froze stays byte-identical. Nothing on disk is rewritten: a span line is durable history, and the second execution genuinely happened. Re-folded from the real lines and the real authority row, `00419600` serves 14 events / 14 distinct ids with its two `Timecard Search` executions measured separately (30.18 s, 30.48 s). | ref dashboard-surfaces §2.3 "D258" (primary) · `src/server/dashboard/queries/run-detail.ts` · `tests/unit/server/run-detail-timeline-collision.test.ts` · `docs/rebuild/migrations/separations.md` §9.22 item 6 |
| **D259** | **A REPLAYED STEP IS ITS OWN TIMELINE ARM, AND IT IS NEVER `pending`.** Replayed nodes emit no spans by design (ref workflow-model §5.3) and the run-detail timeline folds spans, so replay was invisible to it: a terminal run served `steps[].state = "pending"` for every node it replayed, and Track C watched `Identity check — pending, took 0s` on the category whose gate the operator had approved a minute earlier (separations §9.22 item 3). `pending` means *this has not happened yet* — false twice over, because it did happen and its result is the one the run consumed. The reader MOVES (D144's shape a fifth time) and nothing fabricates a span: `TimelineStepWire.state` gains `replayed`, and a replayed member carries `replayedFrom` — the stored checkpoint's own `attempt` and, when the row names one, its `claimGeneration`; a pre-D214 row's absent generation is omitted, never fabricated as `1`. A replayed member carries NO duration, and the schema refuses one: a replay emits no span, so `0` would read as "it ran instantly", which is the `took 0s` this arm exists to delete. Three conditions keep the claim TRUE — the run is terminal `done` (only then did the executor visit every node, so "no span" cannot mean "the run stopped short"), a checkpoint exists at the item-scoped `(workflow, itemId, nodeId)` the planner itself reads, and a DIFFERENT run wrote it (a row this run wrote is evidence of execution here). A category is `replayed` only when every member is; a settled gate is untouched, because a gate never replays across runs. Two residuals are named with their triggers rather than hidden: a branch-excluded or condition-skipped node whose earlier run DID execute it lands in the same arm (closed when D114's terminal `not-run` reasons reach this query), and a node whose spans were pruned stays `pending`. | ref dashboard-surfaces §9.3 (D259, primary) · `src/domain/dashboard/run-detail.ts` · `src/server/dashboard/queries/run-detail.ts` · `src/dashboard/surfaces/log-panel.tsx` · `docs/rebuild/migrations/separations.md` §9.22 item 3 |

## Reconciliation round 84 (2026-08-20) — the separations start door, refused with its trigger named

_phase: 3b_

_D260 is minted by this round and lands with its normative text in its owning doc, ref workflow-model §1.1.
(D256–D259 are minted concurrently by sibling slices of the same wave.)_

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D260** | **A TYPED-TEXT START SURFACE CARRIES EXACTLY ONE VARYING VALUE PER RUN, AND `separations` NEEDS TWO — SO ITS START DOOR IS REFUSED RATHER THAN INVENTED.** Track C (§9.22) recorded the composed workflow as *not startable* from the run modal and the operator ruled *build it*; the build is structurally unavailable and the reason belongs in the rule, not in one descriptor's comment. `EntryRule.emit` is a SINGLE input field and `parseInputRunText` composes one entry into `[{field: rule.emit, value: <the entry text>}, ...constants]`, so `constants[]` can only carry a value that does not vary per run — a per-run value placed there is a constant baked into a bundle-safe descriptor. **A workflow is `inputRun`-startable iff its input schema has exactly one required field that varies per run**, which is a property of the SCHEMA rather than a declaration a descriptor may choose. `SeparationsInput` has three required fields and two of them vary: `documentNumber` is the run's own claim, proved against the level-1 heading, and dropping it deletes the check that catches a stale action id (an `actionId` names a workflow STEP, not a document); `actionId` is the only address Kuali offers, and its reverse lookup is the Action List, whose `actionList.*` family the Kuali store deliberately never ported. **No derivation can close the gap where the gap is**: `services/start/plan.ts` runs exactly ONE ingress parse per raw submission with no I/O and no per-workflow enrichment seam, and an ingress that could resolve a value it was not given is the second unaudited ingress path `ingress.ts` refuses by name — while any document→action lookup is a browser act, one node past where the input schema is parsed. Reversing the ratified `timekeeperName`-is-ingress decision (§9.16 6a-2) reaches TWO varying values, not one, so it is not proposed. The deferral ends when a run surface carries more than one typed value per run; **two shapes are named with their costs** (a third `surfaces` arm over the existing `UploadFieldSpec` vocabulary — whose honest cost is that no client renderer for it exists, `uploadRun` being declared by nobody — or making `actionId` a graph output through a ported Action-List lookup, which still leaves `timekeeperName` to be typed), and the operator owns the choice. ## Reconciliation round 85 (2026-08-20) — the start door, built: the graph resolves its own action

_phase: 3b_

_D261 is minted by this round and lands with its normative text in its owning doc, ref workflow-model §1.1.
It SUPERSEDES round 84's disposition without withdrawing D260's rule — read D260 first; this round is
the operator exercising the choice D260 handed them._

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D261** | **THE ACTION ID IS A GRAPH OUTPUT, SO `separations` STARTS FROM ONE TYPED DOCUMENT NUMBER.** D260 measured `separations` as needing TWO varying values per run and therefore as having no typed start box, and named two shapes that would end the deferral. The operator ruled, verbatim: *"document number should work. no need action id."* — which is D260's **shape 2**, and this decision is that build. `SeparationsInput` drops `actionId`; a new head node `resolve-action` binds `kuali/search-separation-action`, which filters the Action List's own search box to one document number and reads the matched row's `href`. **D260's rule is UNCHANGED and is not withdrawn** — a workflow is `inputRun`-startable iff its input schema has exactly one required field that varies per run — and what moved is the SCHEMA the rule is applied to. `timekeeperName` rides in `constants[]`, legitimate only because it does not vary per RUN; its honest scope is per OPERATOR, and the trigger that ends it is a second timekeeper, at which point it becomes D260 shape 1's typed form field rather than a second constant. The read classifies nothing (D201): the driver states what the list showed and a pure fold decides, with four named refusals — `document-not-in-action-list` (a completed step leaves the list; the refusal names the numbers the list DID show), `ambiguous-action-list-match` (two DISTINCT action ids; two LINKS is the ordinary row, since Kuali publishes the number in both `Document #` and `Document Title`), `unreadable-action-link` (no href, or an href not addressing this space's `…/actions/<24 hex>`), and `unexpected-page-state`. Three legacy keys move candidate→ported with provenance and a live pass (`actionList.searchInput()` narrowed off its CSS scope, `actionList.searchGoButton()`, `actionList.docLink()` READ rather than clicked); `actionList.menuItem()` stays candidate because the list's own route replaces the menu click, and `actionList.docLinks()` because enumerating pending documents is candidate discovery nothing here performs. **The fingerprint moved and every stored `separations` checkpoint stops replaying** — the run's identity genuinely changed shape, which is the honest cost §9.22 measured the mechanics of, not a defect. Two schema moves ride along: a UI evidence assertion's `matchedCount` widens from `z.literal(1)` to a positive int with a refinement pinning every `exactly-one-*` assertion to 1 (the Action-List link's honest live count is 2), and the Kuali registry's "at most one document subject-authoritative observation" narrows to PER SCREEN on the argument that already narrowed it once. LIVE-PROVED end to end 2026-08-20: `4502` typed into the run modal, run `1e0b7cc2-173a-438b-954a-2f118175f8fa` / `sp-105225-1e0b`, `resolve-action` answering `6a766ae6f0549046f7f0dc06` in 25.797 s and parking at the identity gate 1 m 52 s after start with 13 checkpoints and ZERO write rows. | ref workflow-model §1.1 "D261" (primary) · `src/domain/ui/kuali-action-list.ts` · `src/domain/contracts/kuali/search-separation-action.ts` · `src/workflows/separations/descriptor.ts` · `src/domain/workflow/ingress.ts` · `docs/rebuild/migrations/separations.md` §9.24 |

## Reconciliation round 86 (2026-08-20) — the needs-you moment, produced

_phase: 3b_

_D262 is minted by this round and lands with its normative text in its owning doc, ref dashboard-surfaces §2.6.
It is D222's finding a second time — machinery with zero producers — on the trigger this program
cares most about._

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D262** | **A GATE PARK IS THE PRODUCT'S ONE NEEDS-YOU MOMENT, AND IT IS PUBLISHED BY A TRANSITION-WATCHER OVER AUTHORITY, NOT BY THE PARK'S OWN TRANSACTION.** Ref dashboard-surfaces §2.6 has routed the open operator gate onto `gate-waiting` since D79b, `NotificationTriggerEventSchema` has carried the `gate-opened` arm since D138, and the bell has rendered its rows since 2i-B — and **no production caller ever published one**, so the ONE state that cannot move without a person produced no alert at all and D222's backup watch was the inbox's only producer. **No new trigger arm was minted: the union was complete and the producer was missing**, which is exactly D222's shape one trigger over. The seam is the decision. The park is written by the WORKER (ref workflow-model §4's gate arm), a separate OS process with no operator attached and no desktop channel, and D82's rule stands — authority must never depend on a projection to settle a run, so a run must park even when the inbox cannot be written. What makes a watcher sound rather than merely convenient is that **a park is a STANDING authority fact**: `parked-gate` does not go away until a human resolves it, so the waiting set is re-derivable from authority at any later instant by any process — **strictly stronger than atomicity**, which would cover only parks occurring after the producer ships while re-derivation also covers a run already parked, one parked while the dashboard was down, and one parked while the inbox write was failing. The dashboard composition owns it on its own timer (a notification cadence is not the span projection's to decide, and a run waiting on a person must not wait the backup cadence's quarter hour), reading `runs.listParkedAtGate()` — an index scan on the column `tasks_heartbeat_idx` already leads with. Four rules bound it, the first two D222's exactly: a **transition** publishes and a standing park does not (the D134 fold would mark the row unread every tick — §2.6's spam by another route); a **failed publish forgets nothing**, the memory advancing only after the durable row commits, with **no served failure arm and none needed** because the park is still `Waiting` on the queue with its gate named and an unwritable authority is already the `StorageBanner`'s by D222's structural argument; a **resolved gate publishes nothing and rewrites nothing** — the union has no "resolved" arm, announcing an act the operator just performed is the spam §2.6 forbids, and marking the row read would erase the record that a human was ever asked (read state is the operator's own axis, which D138 keeps independent of even snooze) — the park is FORGOTTEN instead, so a re-park is a transition again; and **nothing from the gate's evidence packet travels**, the event being `runId`/`workflowId`/`gateId`, because the packet is exactly the HR data the closed-argument rule exists to keep out. One wire move rides along: `link` joins the notification wire and **only** `link`, on the union's own line — **ids and closed codes may cross to a surface, prose may not** — because `NotificationRowExtras.link` and `NotificationBell`'s `onOpenRun` had been built since 2i-B and were structurally unreachable, leaving a needs-you alert whose sole affordance was "mark read". A `storage` row names no run and draws no button; the button's word stays in the surface, since the server says which run and never what to call the trip. | ref dashboard-surfaces §2.6 (primary, as-built block) · `src/services/notifications/gate-park.ts` · `src/server/dashboard/compose.ts` · `src/infra/sqlite/authority-database.ts` (`runs.listParkedAtGate`) · `src/domain/dashboard/projection.ts` · `src/dashboard/surfaces/top-bar.tsx` |

_phase: 3a_

|---|---|---|
| **D267** | **ONE DUO CEREMONY AT A TIME PER CREDENTIAL, DURABLE IN AUTHORITY, WITH AN UNFORGEABLE TOKEN THE SSO HELPERS REQUIRE.** `duo_ceremony_leases` (schema v16) is keyed on the credential IDENTITY — a digest of the Duo Autopilot credential file's public `(rpId, credentialId)` pairs resolved through `domain/secrets.ts`, never a secret byte, never a per-worker key; a hands-on deployment keys on the configured actor. TTL = `timeouts.sessionAcquireMs` + the new generation-3 leaf `timeouts.duoCeremonyLeaseGraceMs` (D133 read-forward). `defineVerifiedLoginRecipe` is `{navigate, ceremony}` and the ONE acquirer: the ceremony half runs only inside the gate's `withCeremony`, which hands over a `DuoCeremonyLeaseToken` minted and retired only by the lease store (module-private `WeakSet`s, `MutationCapability`'s shape) and asserted by every SSO helper; a warm profile never takes the lease. Waiters are bounded to the remaining envelope minus the grace and refuse by NAME (`DuoCeremonyLeaseHeldError`, no retry loop); a holder past its TTL is taken over at the next generation, its in-flight ceremony cancelled, and a superseded release thrown loud. Duo's terminal refusal (`DuoTerminalRefusalError`) triggers the fresh-profile-once recovery under the HELD lease — proven close, the same profile path removed and recreated, one retry; the second refusal fails naming the directory. Guard `duo-ceremony-lease` with the pre-lease recipe as its violating fixture; proof `tests/unit/duo-ceremony-gate.test.ts`. ## Reconciliation round 92 (2026-08-21) — one status derivation, one task-outcome vocabulary

_phase: 3a_

_D268 is minted by this round and lands with its normative text in its owning doc, ref dashboard-surfaces §2.3._

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D268** | **ONE RUN-STATUS DERIVATION AND ONE TASK-OUTCOME VOCABULARY FOR EVERY DASHBOARD SURFACE (Track D row D1).** Ref dashboard-surfaces §2.3 has always said there is one projection; there were two derivations on top of it. The projector's `#status` joined the span fold's `ended` and `claimedBy` to the authority lifecycle while `queries/run-detail.ts`'s private `statusOf` had NEITHER arm and inlined its own copy of the outcome map, so a run whose `span.ended` folded before its terminal transaction committed read `done` in its queue row and `queued` in the panel that row opens. One level down, `TASK_OUTCOME_PIPELINE` mapped `cancelled|discarded|superseded` to `blocked` while `TASK_OUTCOME_STEP` mapped them to `cancelled`: one span, two words. `deriveRunStatus`, `taskStateOf` and `panelKindOf` are now ONE implementation each in `src/server/dashboard/run-status.ts`, read by both. The unified task vocabulary keeps **`cancelled`** on D123's rule — a word names what happened, and all three of those outcomes name an act somebody took, while `blocked` named none and collided with `interrupted`, the one outcome that reaches no verdict — so `PipelineStepWireSchema.state` gains its `cancelled` arm. The module is a SIBLING of the projector, not part of it: `queries/archive.ts` value-imports `queries/run-detail.ts` and the projector value-reaches `src/base/workflow.ts`, so exporting from there would drag the workflow registry into the archived-run read path `archive-invariants` refuses (D179). | ref dashboard-surfaces §2.3 (primary) · ref frontend-backend-integration §5.1.3 + §7.4 (Track D row D1) · doc 03a §Track D · `docs/rebuild/findings/dashboard-projection.md` |

## Reconciliation round 93 (2026-08-21) — the worker pool's CAS basis and the run session

_phase: 3a_

_D269 is minted by this round and lands with its normative text in its owning doc, ref execution-and-sessions §11._

|---|---|---|
| **D269** | **THE WORKER POOL IS A DURABLE PER-WORKFLOW DECLARATION WITH A CAS VERSION, AND A RUN SESSION IS A DURABLE MEMBER-LESS GROUP WHOSE MEMBERSHIP IS THE RUN ROW.** Schema v17: `worker_pools` (one row per workflow, `{desiredWorkers, plannedBy, plannedAt, version}`, updated under CAS, never deleted — a workflow with NO row is at version 0, nothing seeds one, and `plan-worker-count` / `spawn-worker`'s `expectedVersion` is that pool version, `NonNegativeInt`; the kernel refuses a stale basis with `conflict` naming the current version BEFORE the runtime seam, and `conflict.currentVersion` / `versionChanges.before` admit 0 for this one target), `run_sessions` (insert-only by trigger, `{sessionId, workflow, origin, startedAt}`, no member list), and `tasks.session_id` (mirrored, nullable, indexed; `RunRecord.sessionId` read forward per D133). Every `fanOutQueuedRuns` is ONE session, N ≥ 1, written before its first member in the caller's transaction; `runs.enqueue` refuses a member naming a session authority never wrote; the four callers name the origin (`start-plan` / `intake-manifest` / `archive-relaunch` with the actor; `capture-handoff` with no actor, its id folded from the stable key); a retry INHERITS the retried run's session (D221); D235's bulk strip produces no session. The day wire serves `workerPools[]` (authority rows only; absence = version 0; snapshot-level like `workerCards`, the D4 patch carries rows only) and each queue row its `session` (`present`/`absent` with reason). Unbuilt and still refused by name: the pool process (spawn, pool-indexed ids), the FE view, the parallel proof. ## Reconciliation round 94 (2026-08-21) — the observed log, the emission half

_phase: 3a_

_D270 is minted by this round and lands with its normative text in its owning docs, ref dashboard-surfaces §1.1 and ref operator-trust §2.3.9. D131 stays retired and is never reused._

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D270** | **EVERY RECORDED DATA POINT IS EMITTED ON THE SPAN AT RECORD TIME, REDACTED BY THE DECLARED CLASSIFICATION — AN UNDECLARED LEAF FAILS LOUD.** The D115 collector's points validated a task's output and were then DISCARDED, so the log beside them carried prose about a step rather than the step's evidence. A twelfth span arm, `data.observed`, lands in BOTH halves of the parity pair (`src/core/span-emitter.ts` and the wire leaf `src/events/types.ts`) carrying `{nodeId, taskId, observedAt, points}` on the producing task's own span path — one event per `ctx.recordData` CALL, because a batch is one observation of the page, so the collector reads the clock ONCE per call and `observedAt` is that single instant. RECORD time and not finalization is load-bearing: a task that throws after reading the page has still read the page. Each point's value is split by the provenance collector's own leaf rule and pushed through `redactEvidenceLeaf` — the SAME declared table the D82 receipt uses, never a second implementation — so an UNDECLARED leaf fails the recording task by name, fixtures included (the executor's fixture contracts borrow a manifest-known id because `resolveTaskImplFingerprint` refuses any other, and their paths are declared rather than exempted; a bypass would mean the refusal is never exercised by a composed run). The point carries `direction` NATIVELY and NO `system` (D252/D106 — the store declares it and `dataOrigins` serves it). The channel is the SPAN stream and not notes: the notes stream has no production emitter and `NoteFieldWire`'s closed kinds cannot carry a direction, so `assertNoteWithinRunScope` is not on this path. The queue projection ignores the arm BY NAME, so a stream carrying it cannot degrade `ingestFromDisk` (D218). D184's orphan-classification rule re-baselines on the BUILD MANIFEST's task set rather than the descriptor-reachable one, because the emission fires for whatever a composed descriptor names. Thirteen `recordData` sites stay unclassified BY NAME (four write-sequencer probe roles, four verify roles, four descriptor-less data-service tasks, and one descriptor-less OCR task) — listed in ref operator-trust §2.3.9, never guessed. Commits `ef090ea4` `68954117` `4d074886` `aabd46f4`. | ref dashboard-surfaces §1.1 (the arm) · ref operator-trust §2.3.9 (the declaration) · doc 03a §Observed logs — the log carries what the page said |

## Reconciliation round 109 (2026-09-01) — desktop delivery and update boundary

_phase: 6_
_D297 records the operator's direction after reviewing whether an offline local app should remain an
Electron app and how installed copies receive updates. Its normative text lands in doc 06
§Packaging and its controlled-updater trigger is indexed in doc 04 in this same commit._

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D297** | **KEEP ELECTRON; SHIP V1 WITH SIGNED, IN-PLACE MANUAL UPDATES; PUT A CONTROLLED UPDATER BEHIND ONE FAIL-LOUD QUIESCENCE VERDICT.** Electron owns the installed app's BFF, workers, full Playwright Chromium, Duo extension, keychain boundary and ordered shutdown; offline/local operation does not remove that process-host responsibility. Updating one installed machine means running the newer signed installer over the existing installation without uninstalling or clearing app data. Replaceable program resources and mutable authority/app data stay separate; authority rows, artifacts, profiles, settings and keychain credentials survive a proved older→newer upgrade on macOS and Windows. A later updater may discover and download a signed release while open, but may not install, restart or force-quit while any run is non-terminal or any worker/browser child is active. It installs only after one explicit quiescent verdict and ordered shutdown stop children and the BFF, close storage handles and release locks; active work refuses by name. The optional release channel carries update metadata and binaries, never HR data or credentials. | doc 06 §Packaging (primary) · doc 04 `{#def-desktop-update}` |

_Reconciliation round 110, 2026-09-01. The Workflow Explorer's graph panel spends less of itself on
repetition: one systems chip, no duplicated `category`, one Presentation row, and Scenarios as a
table. Its normative text lands in `ref operator-trust` §5.2.2 in this same commit._

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D299** | **THE EXPLORER'S GRAPH PANEL DRAWS EACH FACT ONCE.** The identity band carries ONE `systems` chip for the whole set and no `category` chip, because `category` is the same value the editable Presentation row serves and the second `system` chip broke the band's one-row rule. The three Presentation fields become one wrapping row of inline label/value pairs; the row prints the short field name (`Label` · `Icon` · `Category`) and the edit door keeps `Edit workflow <field>` as its accessible name (D283), with the 44px floor moved to the coarse-pointer arm. Scenarios becomes a two-column table — the full scenario id, then the file that walks it under a `Walked by` head — where a RESOLVED file prints its base name and carries the whole path on the cell, and an UNRESOLVED one keeps its warning chip and prints the attempted path whole. A workflow with no registered scenario states that in a sentence instead of heading an empty region. No fact is removed and no wire field is added or dropped. | `ref operator-trust` §5.2.2 (primary) |

_Reconciliation round 111, 2026-09-01. The Run Modal's `Import a spreadsheet` arm stops describing
the intake and becomes it, and the workspace's own upload page is deleted. Its normative text lands
in `ref dashboard-surfaces` §9.1 in this same commit._

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D298** | **THE SPREADSHEET ARM IS THE INTAKE'S DROPZONE; `OPEN THE INTAKE…` AND THE WORKSPACE'S UPLOAD PAGE ARE BOTH DELETED.** D296 items 2 and 3 are AMENDED. Item 3 refused a picker in the dialog because one would land the operator "past the mapping step"; the workspace's FIRST screen is that same dropzone, and a drop skips nothing — the upload opens a session and the mapping grid is what comes up. So the Input stage draws the intake's own `Dropzone` from the workspace's own accept/hint/label constants, the drop IS the press (`POST /intake/files`, then the dialog closes on `?intake=<sessionId>` for the workflow THE PICKER selected, file already read), and the arm carries NO primary — `Cancel` alone. A refusal prints the server's code and sentence with "no session was opened"; a transport failure says the file never landed; neither hands the operator over. `IntakeWorkspace` now takes a REQUIRED `sessionId` and loses its `empty`/`refused` states, its `Dropzone`, its `upload` callback and its `Another file` reset, so `?intake=` is always a session id and the `INTAKE_NEW` sentinel retires from `url-state.ts`; a stale `?intake=new` is a session the server does not have and is refused by name. The workflow label moves from the deleted upload label to the workspace header's `meta` slot. **D170/D173 are UNAMENDED: mapping, per-row review, the manifest, the rerun diff and the enqueue all stay in the workspace, because the session is durable and a modal would trap a task that takes minutes.** The `intake-upload` parity state retires into `start-run-spreadsheet`. | ref dashboard-surfaces §9.1 (primary) |

## Reconciliation round 114 (2026-09-01) — the Explorer's node contract as arm cards

_phase: 03a_
_D302 retires the node panel's `Field · System · Recorded` table for one card per task arm. Its
normative text lands in `ref operator-trust` §5.9 in this same commit._

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D302** | **THE EXPLORER'S NODE CONTRACT IS ONE CARD PER TASK ARM, AND A SIDE'S `Recorded` FACT IS STATED ONCE ON ITS GROUP HEAD.** The `Field · System · Recorded` table is retired. The demo's third column carries what ONE RUN recorded, which varies per row; this tree lays no run over the graph (§5.7), so its `Recorded` is the arm's declared POLICY, and a table repeating one policy on every output row said one fact nine times while its rows swung between one and three lines. Each arm is a `Card`: a head (task id, verb, title, and the whole-contract chips `no implementation` / `proof` / `checkpoint` / `artifacts`), then an `Inputs · N` group and an `Outputs · N` group. The group head states the side's shared fact — inputs `bound by the graph`; a read arm's outputs `from` / `fresh` / `override` from the arm's DEFAULT policy, or a warning `unknown`; a prepare arm's `not recorded`; a commit arm's `stored in the transaction checkpoint` — and a row draws only its own DEPARTURE from it: one chip per facet a `byField` rule changes, or a warning chip naming a state that contradicts the head. Rows are `<li>` at `--ds-h-row` carrying path (with the D275 pencil), deviation chips and kind; the `in ·`/`out ·` prefix is gone because the group head is the side. The `System` column is gone: `nodeBindings` already puts every system an arm binds on the node's chip row. Every `ExplorerFieldRecordedWireSchema` arm keeps a slot; no wire field is added or dropped. Ledger 3 rows EX102–EX106, EX108, EX110 and EX112 are superseded. | `ref operator-trust` §5.9 (primary); `ref dashboard-surfaces` §"D227" (pointer) |

|---|---|---|
| **D303** | **THE UI CATALOG IS A RAIL OF IDS BESIDE ONE RECORD, AND THE RAIL KEEPS THE DATE WHILE THE RECORD KEEPS THE REFS.** The Selectors view drew every entry as a seven-line block of identical chips — five of 118 on a screen, `system` / `owner` / both evidence refs repeated on every row of a system, screens flattened beside the elements that live on them — and the operator ruled it unfollowable. It is now the Explorer's shape at system scope (ref dashboard-surfaces §9.5.3): a `listbox` rail grouped by system with every non-screen entry nested under the screen its `screen` field names (an entry whose screen the catalog does not serve throws by id), beside a record `article` that prints the selected entry whole — kind, id, label, the fact chips, `verifiedAt`, both evidence refs, owner, every scenario by name, every task by name, the search terms, and the other entries on its screen as selectable chips. The rail row carries the id, the label and `verifiedAt` at the id's weight; a nested row prints its id without the screen's prefix when it has one (display only — the row's title and the record print it whole). The search keeps a screen as a heading whenever one of its entries matches without counting it, and the record opens on the term's first MATCH, never on a heading. Selection is local, as the Explorer's node selection is: a request is honoured while the rail draws it or while the term it was made under stands, and a new term retires it. §5.8's "both evidence refs on the same line as the id" is bent to "on the record, one click away, never collapsed" — the operator chose this over a two-line ledger (A) that kept the refs beside every id, and over per-screen sheets (C). | `ref operator-trust` §5.8 (primary); `ref dashboard-surfaces` §9.5.3 (pointer) |

Every remaining fact keeps its served words but leaves the `Chip`: a `FactLine` draws `label value` pairs on a WRAPPING line, values in mono, joined by middle dots, because the record's values are catalog ids, error codes and source paths — strings a single-line token was cutting to `…` and recovering on `title` alone. UI ids group by kind in the schema's own order with the kind named once per line; `store kuali · driver kuali` names the driver only when it is not the store's name. `Write safety` alone keeps its chips, because its warning tones carry the fact; an UNRESOLVED source keeps its warning chip because the miss is the exception. `FactRow`'s label becomes a caps group label in a 7rem column across the whole node panel, and the node-level rows (`Depends on`, `Replay`/`Write sequence`, `Detail projections`, `Coverage points`) move ABOVE the arm cards so the panel reads node facts, then arms. No wire field is added or dropped. Ledger 3 rows EX125 and EX136–EX147 are superseded. | `ref operator-trust` §5.9 (primary) |

## Reconciliation round 119 (2026-09-02) — Separations identity authority follows EID origin

_phase: 3b_

|---|---|---|
| **D309** | **AN INDEPENDENTLY SUPPLIED EID THAT EXACTLY MATCHES UCPATH'S ACTUAL DETAIL READ-BACK PROCEEDS WITH MACHINE-VERIFICATION AUTHORITY; AN EID LEARNED FROM A NAME SEARCH ALWAYS REQUIRES THE REAL OPERATOR IDENTITY GATE.** This supersedes D77's unconditional trigger for Separations. `document-eid` and declared `canonical-eid` are eligible only after strict source consistency, packet-digest validation and exact UCPath read-back; missing, malformed, moved, mismatched or unproved supplied identity refuses with no name fallback. `name-resolved` cannot self-qualify even with one exact candidate: it parks at the existing typed-EID ceremony and only the command kernel may record the operator result. A false declarative gate condition opens no gate and creates no operator fact; provenance and receipts re-evaluate it, record the gate obligation absent/non-mandatory, and reject any stale result/checkpoint collision. The resolved subject states `machine-verification` or `operator-approval`, and fresh transaction subject binding plus every business rule remains required. Ordinary Separations gains no repeated per-write gates; D287's extra in-workflow per-write approval requirement is superseded for the supplied-EID verified path. Explicit operational authorization/admission is still required before any `dryRun=false` run, but it is not a workflow gate. ## Reconciliation round 121 (2026-09-02) — one workflow definition, compiled to the proven runtime

_phase: 3c_

consequences land in their respective owning references. No compiler or workflow conversion is
claimed by this documentation decision._

|---|---|---|

Task input references—not tuple position—derive execution dependencies, with duplicates, missing references and cycles refused. The compiler targets the current executor, checkpoints, gates, receipts, write sequencer and wire; D311 creates no second runtime and changes no durable format. D265's workflow folders remain organization/audit authority but no longer define semantic timeline order. Person lookup is the smallest equivalence conversion and separations the completeness conversion; after both preserve current public behavior, redundant hand-authored declarations and duplicate generic-surface tests are deleted. Explorer renders the same compiled tuple model. Already-built run modal, queue, detail, archive, activity, notifications and Explorer behavior is generic: a later workflow gets a separate dashboard task and T2/T3 proof only when it introduces a genuinely new interaction or wire arm. T1 behavior, live selector verification, dry-run, controlled-write and all safety gates remain per workflow where applicable. D317 supplies the final technical names. | ref workflow-model §1.6 (primary) · ref operator-trust §5.2.3 · ref frontend-backend-integration §6.8 · ref guards-and-tests §10.11 · doc 03c §Workflow authoring model |

## Reconciliation round 122 (2026-09-02) — the workflow has a measurable simplicity and recovery contract

_phase: 3c_
_D312–D316 record the operator's answers to the modular-workflow decision brief. The operator
context is consolidated in `docs/decisions/workflow-authoring.md`; normative contracts
land in their owning references and doc 03c in this same commit. These are documentation decisions:
no compiler, conversion, omission UI or recovery command is claimed built._

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D312** | **A REUSE-ONLY WORKFLOW HAS EXACTLY FOUR AUTHORED WORKFLOW-SPECIFIC ARTIFACTS.** They are `src/workflows/<id>/workflow.ts`, its T1 structural scenario, its live dry-run declaration and `docs/rebuild/migrations/<id>.md`. Generated descriptor/module/registry/build-manifest/dashboard projections do not count because authors do not edit them. A genuinely new selector/driver operation, reusable task or dashboard capability may add the files that behavior owns; descriptor/module/registry glue under another name is forbidden. Grouping, labels, run options and omission forms change `workflow.ts` once. Codex, Claude and developers share this authoring contract. | ref workflow-model §1.6.1 (primary) · ref guards-and-tests §10.11 · doc 03c §Workflow authoring model · answered decision sheet item 1 |
| **D313** | **THE RUN MODAL IS THE COMPLETE AUTHORITY FOR OMITTING A WHOLE STEP AND SUPPLYING ITS REPLACEMENT VALUES.** An omitted step executes none of its entries. Its workflow definition declares a complete operator-suppliable value schema; every required replacement value must be present and schema-valid before a start plan can exist, and no worker requests those values mid-run. Replacement values remain typed operator assertions, not observations. A separately declared mandatory verification entry may consume and verify them downstream and remains visible/selected; it is not part of the omitted step. This supersedes D307's same-step-gate exception in the compiled workflow path; D307 remains as-built until conversion. Different omissions or values require a new start plan. | ref workflow-model §5.8 (primary) · ref dashboard-surfaces §9.1 · ref frontend-backend-integration §5.1.1 · ref guards-and-tests §10.11 · answered decision sheet item 2 |
| **D314** | **A FAILED RUN OFFERS RESUME FROM CHECKPOINTS OR RESTART FROM START, AND NEITHER MAY REPEAT A COMPLETED EXTERNAL WRITE.** Resume is the existing failed-node/checkpoint path, including independent failed-parallel-leaf retry. Restart mints the same logical item's next attempt from the graph root, ignores old read/compute checkpoints and reruns those entries. A completed transaction validates durable proof and reconstructs its output without prepare/commit; an unfinished intent probes first. Fresh facts that disagree with a completed write's subject, permanent key or frozen input refuse/park. Unknown writes retain only evidence-resolution actions, and neither recovery command changes input or omissions. | ref workflow-model §5.6.1 (primary) · ref dashboard-surfaces §2.4 · ref frontend-backend-integration §5.1.1 · ref guards-and-tests §10.11 · answered decision sheet item 3 |
| **D315** | **THE TIMELINE TUPLE'S TASK ARRAY USES ONE CLOSED EXPLICIT ENTRY ALGEBRA: `task`, `parallel`, `delegate`, `gate`, OR `transaction`.** Every entry and leaf has a stable id. Parallel declares join/failure policy and checkpoints leaves independently. Delegate compiles to the existing typed child-run edge and its result/retry/cancel policies, never an in-process call. Gates and transactions retain their existing authority boundaries. Typed inputs may derive dependency edges, but the compiler may not infer whether behavior is parallel, delegated, gated or transactional. | ref workflow-model §1.6.1 (primary) · ref guards-and-tests §10.11 · answered decision sheet item 4 |
| **D316** | **PERSON LOOKUP AND SEPARATIONS MUST PROVE PUBLIC EQUIVALENCE, THEN THE SUPERSEDED AUTHORING PATH IS DELETED.** Person Lookup proves the minimum workflow definition; Separations proves grouped steps, rich reads, computations, omissions, conditional gates, retries and transactions. Registry-wide conformance proves every explicit entry kind, including parallel and delegate. Both workflows must preserve current descriptor/module/wire behavior. Acceptance requires removal of redundant hand-authored declarations and duplicate generic-surface tests, no advanced escape hatch, one-file `workflow.ts` changes for grouping/options and D312's four-artifact budget for a reuse-only workflow. | ref workflow-model §1.6.1 (primary) · ref guards-and-tests §10.11 · doc 03c §Workflow authoring model · doc 03b D311/D312–D316 reconciliation · answered decision sheet item 5 |

Freshness is judged by authority generation alone (`current` when the newest verified backup captured the live generation, `stale` when the authority moved past it, `missing` when none exists); the 45-minute age bound is gone and `maxBackupAgeMs` is an optional policy no production composition sets. `storage doctor` exits 0 on healthy authority plus reconciled span authority and reports the backup arm without gating on it. The dashboard's doctor-wire `service` arm is the fixed `never-run` reading naming the command. | ref dashboard-surfaces §2.5 (primary) · `src/server/storage/compose.ts` · `src/server/dashboard/compose.ts` |

## Reconciliation round 140 (2026-09-05) — Retry and Rerun use one authoritative new-run modal

_phase: 1.6_
_D334 records the operator's Task 1.6.4 correction after feasibility inspection exposed that the
current Edit Data command mutates an existing run while the new lifecycle boundary permits only the
service to write it. The decision also resolves the older start-plan/run-identity contradiction. Its
normative behavior lands in ref dashboard-surfaces and ref data-intake-and-edit-data in this same
commit; later consumer and dashboard tasks are rebound, but no product implementation is claimed.
D335 below supersedes the two labels with one Rerun action and specifies defaults, warning-to-Values navigation and explicitly authorized completed-transaction repetition._

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D334** | **RETRY AND RERUN ARE TWO CONTEXTUAL LABELS FOR ONE NEW-RUN MODAL, NEVER COMMANDS THAT EDIT THE SOURCE RUN.** A failed or interrupted run offers **Retry**; a completed run offers **Rerun**. Either opens the ordinary Run modal at Steps. Values from completed steps are available as editable prefill only: the operator may copy, change or clear any value and may select or omit any eligible step. The submitted modal values and omissions are the new run's authoritative typed input/assertions; no prior checkpoint or transaction result is silently inherited, and an omitted completed transaction does not run again or become proof that it ran in the new attempt. The source run remains byte-identical. Confirming the modal consumes one server plan and creates a new queued run; `runId` and `traceId` are minted only in that create transaction, never in the proposal. A parked or uncertain write still exposes only its evidence-resolution actions. D334 supersedes D166's in-place `edit-checkpoint` mutation and D314's no-input/no-omission dual recovery commands, preserves D313's complete modal authority and every write-safety fence, and assigns the backend command/service cutover to 1.6.4, completed-value/provenance reads to 2.6 Task 1-2, and the shared Retry/Rerun modal to 3.1 Task 11-2; 3.1 Task 10-4 retains intake only. | ref dashboard-surfaces §2.4 / §9.1 / §9.3.1 (primary) · ref data-intake-and-edit-data §6 · ref frontend-backend-integration §5.1.1 / §5.1.7 · docs 01 Task 4 · 02 Task 1-2 · 03.1 Tasks 10-4/11-2 |


## Reconciliation round 141 (2026-09-05) — explicit transaction repetition is warned, not prohibited

_phase: 1.6 planning correction; implementation remains open_

| # | Decision | Owning doc |
|---|---|---|
| **D335** | **ONE RERUN ACTION; FAILED SOURCES DEFAULT TO REMAINING STEPS, SUCCESSFUL SOURCES TO ALL STEPS. WARNING CONFIRM ADVANCES TO VALUES; ONLY FINAL MODAL SUBMISSION STARTS THE RUN.** Rerun opens Steps for both outcomes. The operator may select any eligible steps. Proceeding with a selected previously submitted transaction shows a duplicate-submission warning with Cancel / Confirm: Cancel returns to Steps, Confirm acknowledges and opens Values. Neither consumes a plan, creates a run, mints run/trace ids, publishes or submits a transaction. The operator edits Values, then the final modal submission binds the actual values/omissions and revalidated warning acknowledgment and authorizes a fresh submission, never old-receipt reuse. Unchanged warning facts retain acknowledgment through Values edits; changed warned selection/business keys/source/evidence require renewal. Prior completion never vetoes explicit selection. Exact final-command replay never submits twice; a later deliberate Rerun is a new authorization. Source runs/evidence remain unchanged; omitted transactions produce no new write evidence. Natural-business-key admission spans all occurrences, and old evidence cannot settle a new unknown outcome. This supersedes D334's two contextual labels. Task 1.6.4 owns the backend protocol, source-bound execution and minimum frontend compatibility/reference changes; 3.1 Task 11-2 owns the full modal/warning UI; 2.6 Task 1-2 owns completion/value reads.
